\left\|{O_{X}}\right\|,\left\|{\mathcal{L}_{Y}}\right\|_{\diamond}\leq 1\end{subarray}}\left|{\llangle{O_{X},I_{E}}|\mathcal{U}_{\mathcal{B}}(t,t^{\prime})\mathcal{L}_{Y}|{\phi}\rrangle}\right|,

(77)

where the supremum is taken over system operators
O_{X}
supported on
X\subseteq\Lambda
and system superoperators
\mathcal{L}_{Y}
, supported on
Y
, which additionally satisfies
\mathcal{L}_{Y}^{\dagger}(I)=0
.

A bound on
\gamma_{\bm{\zeta},\mathcal{B}}^{X,Y}(t,t^{\prime})
directly bounds the right-contraction part
\Delta_{1,O_{X}}(t,t^{\prime};l)
in Lemma
5
, which we make precise in the next lemma.

Lemma 8
.

For any
t>t^{\prime}
and
l>0
,
\Delta_{O_{X}}^{\textnormal{R}}
defined in Lemma
5
satisfies

\displaystyle\Delta_{O_{X}}^{\textnormal{R}}(t,t^{\prime};l)\leq 2\left\|{O_{X}}\right\|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\notin\mathcal{A}_{X[l]}\\
\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}\cap X_{c}[l]\neq\emptyset\end{subarray}}\left(1+\mu^{\textnormal{R}}_{\alpha}(0,t)\right)\int_{s=t^{\prime}}^{t}\gamma^{X,\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}}_{\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{R}}_{[0,s]},\mathcal{A}_{X[l]}}(t,s)ds,

(78a)

and
\Delta_{O_{X}}^{\textnormal{L}}
also defined in Lemma
5
is given by

\displaystyle\Delta^{\textnormal{L}}_{O_{X}}(t,t^{\prime};l)\leq 2\left\|{O_{X}}\right\|\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}_{X[l]}}\sum_{\alpha\notin\mathcal{A}_{X[l]},\sigma}\int_{s^{\prime}=t^{\prime}}^{t}\int_{s=t^{\prime}}^{s^{\prime}}|{K_{\alpha^{\prime},\alpha,\sigma}(s^{\prime}-s)}|\gamma^{X,S_{\alpha^{\prime}}}_{\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{R}}_{[0,s^{\prime}]},\mathcal{A}_{X[l]}}(t,s^{\prime})ds^{\prime}ds.

(78b)

Proof.

Since
|{\rho(s)}\rrangle,|{\rho_{\alpha^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}}(s,s^{\prime})}\rrangle
defined in lemma
5
are in the operator space
\mathcal{S}_{\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{R}}_{[0,s]}}(\rho_{E})
and
\|{\mathcal{C}_{h_{\alpha}(s)}}\|_{\diamond}\leq 2\|{h_{\alpha}(s)}\|\leq 2
,

|{\llangle{O_{X},I_{E}}|\mathcal{U}_{X[l]}(t,s)\mathcal{C}_{h_{\alpha}}|{\rho(s)}\rrangle}|,|{\llangle{O_{X},I_{E}}|\mathcal{U}_{X[l]}(t,s)\mathcal{C}_{R_{\alpha}}|{\rho_{\alpha^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}}(s,s^{\prime})}\rrangle}|\leq 2\left\|{O_{X}}\right\|\gamma^{X,\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}}_{\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{R}}_{[0,s]},\mathcal{A}_{X[l]}}(t,s).

Therefore, it follows that

\displaystyle\Delta^{\textnormal{R}}_{O_{X}}(t,t^{\prime};l)\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\notin\mathcal{A}_{X[l]}\\
\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}\cap{X}_{c}[l]\neq\emptyset\end{subarray}}\bigg(2\left\|{O_{X}}\right\|\int_{t^{\prime}}^{t}\gamma^{X,S_{\alpha}}_{\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{R}}_{[0,s]},\mathcal{A}_{X[l]}}(t,s)ds+

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad 2\left\|{O_{X}}\right\|\int_{s=t^{\prime}}^{t}\bigg(\sum_{\alpha^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}}\int_{s^{\prime}=0}^{s}|{K_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}}(s-s^{\prime})}|ds^{\prime}\bigg)\gamma^{X,S_{\alpha}}_{\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{R}}_{[0,s],\mathcal{A}_{X[l]}}}(t,s)ds\bigg),

\displaystyle\leq 2\left\|{O_{X}}\right\|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\notin\mathcal{A}_{X[l]}\\
\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}\cap{X}_{c}[l]\neq\emptyset\end{subarray}}\int_{t^{\prime}}^{t}(1+\mu_{\alpha}^{\textnormal{R}}(0,s))\gamma^{X,S_{\alpha}}_{\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{R}}_{[0,s]},\mathcal{A}_{X[l]}}(t,s)ds.

Finally, using that
\mu_{\alpha}^{\textnormal{R}}(0,s)\leq\mu_{\alpha}^{\textnormal{R}}(0,t)
since
s\leq t
, we obtain the bound on
\Delta^{\textnormal{R}}_{O_{X}}(t,t^{\prime};l)
in the lemma statement. The bound on
\Delta^{\textnormal{L}}_{O_{X}}(t,t^{\prime};l)
follows from a similar analysis by noting that
|{\tilde{\rho}_{\alpha,\sigma}(s,s^{\prime})}\rrangle
defined in lemma
5
is in the operator space
\mathcal{S}_{\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{R}}_{[0,s^{\prime}]}}(\rho_{E})
.
∎

Next, we bring the reader’s attention to two mathematical requirements that are needed to ensure that all the calculations in the following subsection are legitimate. The corresponding lemmas proving that these requirements are satisfied can be found in appendix
B
. If the reader is willing to accept that these manipulations are legitimate and does not want to be encumbered by mathematical details of their proof, then they can take these to be true and skip ahead to the next subsection. These considerations arise uniquely for the problem studied in this paper, and do not arise while deriving Lieb Robinson bounds for finite-dimensional lattice models.

Consideration 1
. In the calculations in the following subsection, we will often use the fact that for a smooth and bounded function
g:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{C}
integrals of the form

\int_{s_{0}^{\prime}}^{s_{1}^{\prime}}g(s)\gamma^{X,Y}_{\bm{\zeta}+\beta_{0}\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{R}}_{[s_{0},s]},\mathcal{B}}(s_{1},s)ds,

where
s_{0}<s_{1}
,
\bm{\zeta}=\{\zeta_{\alpha}\}
with
\zeta_{\alpha}:\mathbb{R}\to[0,\infty)
being locally bounded functions and
\beta_{0}>0
, is well defined. Note that this is already implicit in the bound provided in Eq. (
78
). Furthermore, we will also use the fact that for a smooth and integrable function
k:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{C}
,
h:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{C}
defined by

h(s)=\int_{s_{0}^{\prime}}^{s_{1}^{\prime}}k(s-s^{\prime})\gamma^{X,Y}_{\bm{\zeta}+\beta_{0}\bm{\mu}_{[s_{0},s^{\prime}]}^{R},\mathcal{B}}(s_{1},s^{\prime})ds^{\prime},

is well defined and integrable. Both of these considerations are addressed in lemma
32
in appendix
B
.

Consideration 2
. in the derivation of the Lieb Robinson bound, a key tool that we will use is
lower Lipschitz boundedness
of
\gamma^{X,Y}_{\bm{\zeta}+\beta_{0}\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{R}}_{[s_{0},s]},\mathcal{B}}(s_{1},s)
i.e., on perturbing
s\to s-\varepsilon
in
\gamma^{X,Y}_{\bm{\zeta}+\beta_{0}\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{R}}_{[s_{0},s]},\mathcal{B}}(s_{1},s)
, it does not increase significantly beyond its unperturbed value. More specifically, we would need

\displaystyle\gamma^{X,Y}_{\bm{\zeta}+\beta_{0}\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{R}}_{[s_{0},s-\varepsilon]},\mathcal{B}}(s_{1},s-\varepsilon)\leq\gamma^{X,Y}_{\bm{\zeta}+\beta_{0}\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{R}}_{[s_{0},s]},\mathcal{B}}(s_{1},s)+O(\varepsilon).

(80)

Had
\gamma^{X,Y}_{\bm{\zeta}+\beta_{0}\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{R}}_{[s_{0},\cdot]},\mathcal{B}}(s_{1},\cdot)
been a smooth function, this would simply have followed from its Taylor expansion. However, it is not necessary for
\gamma^{X,Y}_{\bm{\zeta}+\beta_{0}\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{R}}_{[s_{0},\cdot]},\mathcal{B}}(s_{1},\cdot)
to even be a continuous function (even though, as described above and to be proved below, it is integrable). In appendix
B
, by proving the following lemma, we show that despite this non-smoothness, it still satisfies Eq.
80
which will turn out to be enough for deriving a Lieb Robinson bound.

Lemma 9
.

For any
X,Y\subseteq\Lambda
,
s_{0}<s<t
,
\beta_{0}>1
and for
\varepsilon<\left|{s-s_{0}}\right|

\displaystyle\gamma^{X,Y}_{\bm{\zeta}+\beta_{0}\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{R}}_{[s_{0},s-\varepsilon]},\mathcal{B}}(t,s-\varepsilon)\leq\gamma^{X,Y}_{\bm{\zeta}+\beta_{0}\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{R}}_{[s_{0},s]},\mathcal{B}}(t,s)+C\varepsilon

(81)

for some finite number
C
which depends only on
\left|{\mathcal{B}}\right|
,
\|{\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{L}}(-t,t)}\|_{\infty},\|{\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{R}}(-t,t)}\|_{\infty}
and
\|{\bm{\zeta}_{[0,t]}}\|_{\infty}
.

IX.3
Deriving the Lieb-Robinson recursion

To obtain a recursive inequality for the commutator bound for the models under consideration, as we did for the case of bounded lattice Hamiltonians (section
VIII
), we need to estimate the difference between
\gamma^{X,Y}_{\bm{\zeta},\mathcal{B}}(t,t^{\prime}-\varepsilon)
and
\gamma^{X,Y}_{\bm{\zeta},\mathcal{B}}(t,t^{\prime})
. At a high-level, the procedure is similar to that in the case of bounded lattice Hamiltonians: Consider
\llangle{O_{X},I_{E}}|\mathcal{U}_{\mathcal{B}}(t,t^{\prime}-\varepsilon)\mathcal{L}_{Y}|{\phi}\rrangle
for some
|{\phi}\rrangle\in\mathcal{S}_{\bm{\zeta}}(\rho_{E})
and perform the following sets of approximations:

\displaystyle\llangle{O_{X},I_{E}}|\mathcal{U}_{\mathcal{B}}(t,t^{\prime}-\varepsilon)\mathcal{L}_{Y}|{\phi}\rrangle

\displaystyle=\llangle{O_{X},I_{E}}|\mathcal{U}_{\mathcal{B}}(t,t^{\prime})\mathcal{U}_{\mathcal{B}}(t^{\prime},t^{\prime}-\varepsilon)\mathcal{L}_{Y}\mathcal{U}_{\mathcal{B}}(t^{\prime}-\varepsilon,t^{\prime})\mathcal{U}_{\mathcal{B}}(t^{\prime},t^{\prime}-\varepsilon)|{\phi}\rrangle

\displaystyle\approx\llangle{O_{X},I_{E}}|\mathcal{U}_{\mathcal{B}}(t,t^{\prime})\mathcal{U}_{\mathcal{B}\cap\mathcal{A}_{Y}}(t^{\prime},t^{\prime}-\varepsilon)\mathcal{L}_{Y}\mathcal{U}_{\mathcal{B}\cap\mathcal{A}_{Y}}(t^{\prime}-\varepsilon,t^{\prime})\mathcal{U}_{\mathcal{B}}(t^{\prime}-\varepsilon,t^{\prime})|{{\phi}}\rrangle+O(\varepsilon^{2})

\displaystyle\approx\llangle{O_{X},I_{E}}|\mathcal{U}_{\mathcal{B}}(t,t^{\prime})\bigg(I-i\int_{t^{\prime}-\varepsilon}^{t^{\prime}}\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}\cap\mathcal{A}_{Y}}(s)ds\bigg)\mathcal{L}_{Y}\mathcal{U}_{\mathcal{B}\cap\mathcal{A}_{Y}}(t^{\prime}-\varepsilon,t^{\prime})\mathcal{U}_{\mathcal{B}}(t^{\prime}-\varepsilon,t^{\prime})|{{\phi}}\rrangle+O(\varepsilon^{2}).

Now, clearly the state
|{\tilde{\phi}}\rrangle
defined via

|{\tilde{\phi}}\rrangle=\mathcal{U}_{\mathcal{A}_{Y}\cap\mathcal{B}}(t^{\prime}-\varepsilon,t^{\prime})\mathcal{U}_{\mathcal{B}}(t^{\prime}-\varepsilon,t^{\prime})|{\phi}\rrangle\in\mathcal{S}_{\bm{\zeta}+2\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{R}}_{[t^{\prime}-\varepsilon,t^{\prime}]}}(\rho_{E}).

The next lemma makes precise that the above approximations are valid to
O(\varepsilon^{2})
. The subtlety that needs to be handled here is the unbounded system-environment interaction Hamiltonian, which is not present in the case of bounded lattice Hamiltonians.

Lemma 10
.

Suppose
|{\phi}\rrangle\in\mathcal{S}_{\bm{\zeta}}(\rho_{E})
,
\varepsilon<t^{\prime}
,
O_{X}
is an observable supported in region
X
and
\mathcal{L}_{Y}
is a system superoperator supported on the region
Y
, then

\left|{\llangle{O_{X},I_{E}}|\mathcal{U}_{\mathcal{B}}(t,t^{\prime}-\varepsilon)\mathcal{L}_{Y}|{\phi}\rrangle-\llangle{O_{X},I_{E}}|\mathcal{U}_{\mathcal{B}}(t,t^{\prime})\bigg(I-i\int_{t^{\prime}-\varepsilon}^{t^{\prime}}\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}\cap\mathcal{A}_{Y}}(s)ds\bigg)\mathcal{L}_{Y}|{\tilde{\phi}}\rrangle}\right|\leq C_{0}\left\|{\mathcal{L}_{Y}}\right\|_{\diamond}\left\|{O_{X}}\right\|\varepsilon^{2},

for some
|{\tilde{\phi}}\rrangle\in\mathcal{S}_{\bm{\zeta}+2\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{R}}_{[t^{\prime}-\varepsilon,t^{\prime}]}}(\rho_{E})
and for
C_{0}
that depends only on the support of the region
Y
,
\left\|{\bm{\zeta}_{[0,t]}}\right\|_{\infty}
,
\left\|{\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{R}}(-t,t)}\right\|_{\infty}
,
\left\|{\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{L}}(-t,t)}\right\|_{\infty}
and
\left\|{K}\right\|_{\infty}
.

The proof of this lemma is a relatively straightforward but tedious application of a second-order Taylor expansion and is provided in appendix
C
.

We now combine this Taylor expansion estimate with this independent-bath support property to derive one of the key results that we will use repeatedly in proving Lieb-Robinson bound: An inequality relating
\gamma^{X,Y}_{\bm{\zeta},\mathcal{B}}(t,t^{\prime}-\varepsilon)
to
\gamma^{X,Y}_{\bm{\zeta},\mathcal{}}(t,t^{\prime})
.

Lemma 11

(Differential upper bound)
.

For sufficiently small
\varepsilon
and
t^{\prime}<t
,

\displaystyle\gamma_{\bm{\zeta},\mathcal{B}}^{X,Y}(t,t^{\prime}-\varepsilon)-\gamma_{\bm{\zeta}+2\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{R}}_{[t^{\prime}-\varepsilon,t^{\prime}]},\mathcal{B}}^{X,Y}(t,t^{\prime})\leq

\displaystyle\qquad\varepsilon\sum_{\alpha:\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}\cap Y\neq\emptyset}\bigg(2(1+2\left\|{\bm{\zeta}_{[t^{\prime}-\varepsilon,t^{\prime}]}}\right\|_{\infty})\gamma^{X,\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}}_{\bm{\zeta}+2\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{R}}_{[t^{\prime}-\varepsilon,t^{\prime}]},\mathcal{B}}(t,t^{\prime})+2\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B},\sigma}\int_{t^{\prime}}^{t}\left|{K_{\alpha^{\prime},\alpha,\sigma}(s^{\prime}-t^{\prime})}\right|\gamma^{X,\mathcal{S}_{\alpha^{\prime}}}_{\bm{\zeta}+2\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{R}}_{[t^{\prime}-\varepsilon,s^{\prime}]},\mathcal{B}}(t,s^{\prime})ds^{\prime}\bigg)+C_{1}\varepsilon^{2},

(82)

where
C_{1}
depends only on
\left|{\mathcal{B}}\right|
,
\left\|{\bm{\zeta}_{[0,t]}}\right\|_{\infty},\left\|{\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{R}}(-t,t)}\right\|_{\infty},\left\|{\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{L}}(-t,t)}\right\|_{\infty}
and
\left\|{K_{[0,t]}}\right\|_{\infty}
.

Proof.

From lemma
10
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\gamma^{X,Y}_{\bm{\zeta},\mathcal{B}}(t,t^{\prime}-\varepsilon)

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\phi\in\mathcal{S}_{\bm{\zeta}}(\rho_{E})\\
\left\|{O_{X}}\right\|\leq 1,\left\|{\mathcal{L}_{Y}}\right\|_{\diamond}\leq 1\end{subarray}}\left|{\llangle{O_{X},I_{E}}|\mathcal{U}_{\mathcal{B}}(t,t^{\prime})\mathcal{L}_{Y}|{\tilde{\phi}}\rrangle}\right|+\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\phi\in\mathcal{S}_{\bm{\zeta}}(\rho_{E})\\
\left\|{O_{X}}\right\|\leq 1,\left\|{\mathcal{L}_{Y}}\right\|_{\diamond}\leq 1\end{subarray}}\left|{\llangle{O_{X},I_{E}}|\mathcal{U}_{\mathcal{B}}(t,t^{\prime})\bigg(\int_{t^{\prime}-\varepsilon}^{t^{\prime}}\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}_{Y}\cap\mathcal{B}}(s)ds\bigg)\mathcal{L}_{Y}|{\tilde{\phi}}\rrangle}\right|+C_{0}\varepsilon^{2},

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle(\mkern-1.5mu1\mkern-1.5mu)}}{{\leq}}\gamma^{X,Y}_{\bm{\zeta}+2\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{R}}_{[t^{\prime}-\varepsilon,t^{\prime}]},\mathcal{B}}(t,t^{\prime})+T_{\varepsilon}(t,t^{\prime})+C_{0}\varepsilon^{2},

where
C_{0}
is as defined in lemma
10
and depends only
\left|{\mathcal{B}}\right|
,
\left\|{\bm{\zeta}_{[0,t]}}\right\|_{\infty}
,
\left\|{\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{R}}(-t,t)}\right\|_{\infty}
,
\left\|{\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{L}}(-t,t)}\right\|_{\infty}
and
\left\|{K}\right\|_{\infty}
. In the above, in (1) we have used the fact that
|{\tilde{\phi}}\rrangle\in\mathcal{S}_{\bm{\zeta}+2\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{R}}_{[t^{\prime}-\varepsilon,t^{\prime}]}}(\rho_{E})
and defined

\displaystyle T_{\varepsilon}(t,t^{\prime})=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\tilde{\phi}\in\mathcal{S}_{\bm{\zeta}+2\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{R}}_{[t^{\prime}-\varepsilon,t^{\prime}]}}(\rho_{E})\\
\left\|{O_{X}}\right\|\leq 1,\left\|{\mathcal{L}_{Y}}\right\|_{\diamond}\leq 1\end{subarray}}\left|{\llangle{O_{X},I_{E}}|\mathcal{U}_{\mathcal{B}}(t,t^{\prime})\bigg(\int_{t^{\prime}-\varepsilon}^{t^{\prime}}\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}_{Y}\cap\mathcal{B}}(s)ds\bigg)\mathcal{L}_{Y}|{\tilde{\phi}}\rrangle}\right|.

Next, we upperbound
T_{\varepsilon}(t,t^{\prime})
. We start by noting that

\displaystyle\left|{\llangle{O_{X},I_{E}}|\mathcal{U}_{\mathcal{B}}(t,t^{\prime})\bigg(\int_{t^{\prime}-\varepsilon}^{t^{\prime}}\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}_{Y}\cap\mathcal{B}}(s)ds\bigg)\mathcal{L}_{Y}|{\tilde{\phi}}\rrangle}\right|\leq\sum_{\alpha:S_{\alpha}\cap Y\neq\emptyset}\int_{t^{\prime}-\varepsilon}^{t^{\prime}}\left|{\llangle{O_{X},I_{E}}|\mathcal{U}_{\mathcal{B}}(t,t^{\prime})\mathcal{C}_{h_{\alpha}(s)}\mathcal{L}_{Y}|{\tilde{\phi}}\rrangle}\right|ds+

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\sum_{\alpha:S_{\alpha}\cap Y\neq\emptyset}\left|{(-1)^{\sigma}\int_{t^{\prime}-\varepsilon}^{t^{\prime}}\llangle{O_{X},I_{E}}|\mathcal{U}_{\mathcal{B}}(t,t^{\prime})B_{\alpha,\sigma}(s)R_{\alpha,\sigma}(s)\mathcal{L}_{Y}|{\tilde{\phi}}\rrangle ds}\right|\bigg).

(83)

We now analyze the right hand side of Eq.
83
term by term. Note first that,

\displaystyle\int_{t^{\prime}-\varepsilon}^{t^{\prime}}\left|{\llangle{O_{X},I_{E}}|\mathcal{U}_{\mathcal{B}}(t,t^{\prime})\mathcal{C}_{h_{\alpha}(s)}\mathcal{L}_{Y}|{\tilde{\phi}}\rrangle}\right|ds

\displaystyle\leq 2\varepsilon\left\|{\mathcal{L}_{Y}}\right\|_{\diamond}\gamma^{X,\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}}_{\bm{\zeta}+2\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{R}}_{[t^{\prime}-\varepsilon,t^{\prime}]},\mathcal{B}}(t,t^{\prime}),

(84)

since by lemma
6
b,
\mathcal{L}_{Y}|{\tilde{\phi}}\rrangle\in\left\|{\mathcal{L}_{Y}}\right\|_{\diamond}\mathcal{S}_{\bm{\zeta}+2\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{R}}_{[t^{\prime}-\varepsilon,t^{\prime}]}}(\rho_{E})
and
\left\|{\mathcal{C}_{h_{\alpha}}}\right\|_{\diamond}\leq 2\left\|{h_{\alpha}}\right\|\leq 2
. Next, we use the Wick’s theorem to obtain

\displaystyle\left|{\int_{t^{\prime}-\varepsilon}^{t^{\prime}}\llangle{O_{X},I_{E}}|\mathcal{U}_{\mathcal{B}}(t,t^{\prime})B_{\alpha,\sigma}(s)R_{\alpha,\sigma}(s)\mathcal{L}_{Y}|{\tilde{\phi}}\rrangle ds}\right|

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle(\mkern-1.5mu1\mkern-1.5mu)}}{{\leq}}\left|{\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B},\sigma}\int_{s=t^{\prime}-\varepsilon}^{t^{\prime}}\int_{s^{\prime}=t^{\prime}}^{t}K_{\alpha^{\prime},\alpha,\sigma}(s^{\prime}-s)\llangle{O_{X},I_{E}}|\mathcal{U}_{\mathcal{B}}(t,s^{\prime})\mathcal{C}_{R_{\alpha^{\prime}}(s^{\prime})}\mathcal{U}_{\mathcal{B}}(s^{\prime},t^{\prime})R_{\alpha,\sigma}(s)\mathcal{L}_{Y}|{\tilde{\phi}}\rrangle ds^{\prime}ds}\right|+

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\left|{\int_{t^{\prime}-\varepsilon}^{t^{\prime}}\llangle{O_{X},I_{E}}|\mathcal{U}_{\mathcal{B}}(t,t^{\prime})\mathcal{C}_{R_{\alpha}(s)}\mathcal{L}_{Y}\overrightarrow{\text{W}}(B_{\alpha,\sigma}(s),|{\tilde{\phi}}\rrangle)ds}\right|

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle(\mkern-1.5mu2\mkern-1.5mu)}}{{\leq}}2\left\|{\mathcal{L}_{Y}}\right\|_{\diamond}\left\|{O_{X}}\right\|\bigg(\bigg(\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B},\sigma}\int_{s=t^{\prime}-\varepsilon}^{t^{\prime}}\int_{s^{\prime}=t^{\prime}}^{t}\left|{K_{\alpha^{\prime},\alpha,\sigma}(s^{\prime}-s)}\right|\gamma^{X,S_{\alpha^{\prime}}}_{\bm{\zeta}+2\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{R}}_{[t^{\prime}-\varepsilon,s^{\prime}]},\mathcal{B}}(t,s^{\prime})ds^{\prime}ds\bigg)+

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\varepsilon(\left\|{\bm{\zeta}_{[t^{\prime}-\varepsilon,t^{\prime}]}}\right\|_{\infty}+2\sup_{s^{\prime}\in[t^{\prime}-\varepsilon,t^{\prime}]}\left\|{\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{R}}_{[t^{\prime}-\varepsilon,t^{\prime}]}(s^{\prime})}\right\|_{\infty})\gamma_{\bm{\zeta}+2\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{R}}_{[t^{\prime}-\varepsilon,t^{\prime}]},\mathcal{B}}^{X,\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}}(t,t^{\prime})\bigg)\noindent

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle(\mkern-1.5mu3\mkern-1.5mu)}}{{\leq}}\left\|{\mathcal{L}_{Y}}\right\|_{\diamond}\left\|{O_{X}}\right\|\bigg(\bigg(\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B},\sigma}\int_{s=t^{\prime}-\varepsilon}^{t^{\prime}}\int_{s^{\prime}=t^{\prime}}^{t}\left|{K_{\alpha^{\prime},\alpha,\sigma}(s^{\prime}-s)}\right|\gamma^{X,\mathcal{S}_{\alpha^{\prime}}}_{\bm{\zeta}+2\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{R}}_{[t^{\prime}-\varepsilon,s^{\prime}]},\mathcal{B}}(t,s^{\prime})ds^{\prime}ds\bigg)+2\varepsilon\left\|{\bm{\zeta}_{[t^{\prime}-\varepsilon,t^{\prime}]}}\right\|_{\infty}\gamma^{X,\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}}_{\bm{\zeta}+2\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{R}}_{[t^{\prime}-\varepsilon,t^{\prime}]},\mathcal{B}}(t,t^{\prime})+

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad 8\varepsilon^{2}\left|{\mathcal{B}}\right|\left\|{K_{[0,t]}}\right\|_{\infty}\bigg),

(85)

where in (1) we have used the fact that
\overrightarrow{\textnormal{W}}_{\rho_{E}}(B_{\alpha,\sigma}(s),|{\tilde{\phi}}\rrangle)
is independent of
\sigma
to set
(-1)^{\sigma}R_{\alpha,\sigma}(s)\mathcal{L}_{Y}\overrightarrow{\textnormal{W}}_{\rho_{E}}(B_{\alpha,\sigma}(s),|{\tilde{\phi}}\rrangle)=\mathcal{C}_{R_{\alpha}(s)}\mathcal{L}_{Y}\overrightarrow{\textnormal{W}}_{\rho_{E}}(B_{\alpha,l}(s),|{\tilde{\phi}}\rrangle)
. In (2) we have used the closure property in lemma
6
c to upper bound

\left|{\llangle{O_{X},I_{E}}|\mathcal{U}_{\mathcal{B}}(t,t^{\prime})\mathcal{C}_{X_{\alpha}}\mathcal{L}_{Y}\overrightarrow{\text{W}}(B_{\alpha,l}(s),|{\tilde{\phi}}\rrangle)}\right|\leq(\|{\bm{\zeta}(s)}\|_{\infty}+2\|{\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{R}}_{[t^{\prime}-\varepsilon,t^{\prime}]}(s)}\|_{\infty})\left|{\llangle{O_{X},I_{E}}|\mathcal{U}_{\mathcal{B}}(t,t^{\prime})C_{X_{\alpha,\sigma}}\mathcal{L}_{Y}|{\tilde{\phi}}\rrangle}\right|,

and to upper bound
\small{\llangle{O_{X},I_{E}}|\mathcal{U}_{\mathcal{B}}(t,s^{\prime})\mathcal{C}_{R_{\alpha^{\prime}}}\mathcal{U}_{\mathcal{B}}(s^{\prime},t^{\prime})X_{\alpha,\sigma}\mathcal{L}_{Y}|{\tilde{\phi}}\rrangle}
, we have used lemma
6
a to identify that
\mathcal{U}_{\mathcal{B}}(s^{\prime},t^{\prime})X_{\alpha,\sigma}\mathcal{L}_{Y}|{\tilde{\phi}}\rrangle\in\mathcal{S}_{\bm{\zeta}+2\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{R}}_{[t^{\prime}-\varepsilon,t^{\prime}]}+\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{R}}_{[t^{\prime},s^{\prime}]}}(\rho_{E})\subseteq\mathcal{S}_{\bm{\zeta}+2\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{R}}_{[t^{\prime}-\varepsilon,s^{\prime}]}}(\rho_{E})
. In (3) we have used the fact that
\|{\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{R}}_{[t^{\prime}-\varepsilon,t^{\prime}]}(s^{\prime})}\|_{\infty}\leq 2\varepsilon\left|{\mathcal{B}}\right|\left\|{K_{[0,t]}}\right\|_{\infty}
. Note also that in (2), we have implicitly used the result stated in consideration 1 in the previous section (more specifically, lemma
32
b from the appendix
B
) to insure that the double integral is well defined.

Next, we further manipulate the first term in Eq.
85
by first performing a change of variables
s\to s
and
s^{\prime}\to s+\tau
to obtain

\displaystyle\int_{s=t^{\prime}-\varepsilon}^{t^{\prime}}\int_{s^{\prime}=t^{\prime}}^{t}\left|{K_{\alpha^{\prime},\alpha,\sigma}(s^{\prime}-s)}\right|\gamma_{\bm{\zeta}+2\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{R}}_{[t^{\prime}-\varepsilon,s^{\prime}]},\mathcal{B}}^{X,\mathcal{S}_{\alpha^{\prime}}}(t,s^{\prime})ds^{\prime}ds=\int_{s=t^{\prime}-\varepsilon}^{t^{\prime}}\int_{\tau=t^{\prime}-s}^{t-s}\left|{K_{\alpha^{\prime},\alpha,\sigma}(\tau)}\right|\gamma_{\bm{\zeta}+2\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{R}}_{[t^{\prime}-\varepsilon,s+\tau]},\mathcal{B}}^{X,\mathcal{S}_{\alpha^{\prime}}}(t,s+\tau)dsd\tau.

(86)

Next, since the integrand is non-negative, we upper bound the integration region
t^{\prime}-\varepsilon\leq s\leq t^{\prime},t^{\prime}-s\leq\tau\leq t-s
as depicted in Fig.
6
and obtain

\displaystyle\int_{t^{\prime}-\varepsilon}^{t^{\prime}}\int_{t^{\prime}}^{t}\left|{K_{\alpha^{\prime},\alpha,\sigma}(s^{\prime}-s)}\right|\gamma_{\bm{\zeta}+2\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{R}}_{[t^{\prime}-\varepsilon,s^{\prime}]},\mathcal{B}}^{X,\mathcal{S}_{\alpha^{\prime}}}(t,s^{\prime})ds^{\prime}ds

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\int_{\tau=0}^{t-t^{\prime}}\int_{s=t^{\prime}-\varepsilon}^{t^{\prime}}\left|{K_{\alpha^{\prime},\alpha,\sigma}(\tau)}\right|\gamma_{\bm{\zeta}+2\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{R}}_{[t^{\prime}-\varepsilon,s+\tau]},\mathcal{B}}^{X,\mathcal{S}_{\alpha^{\prime}}}(t,s+\tau)dsd\tau+\int_{\tau=t-t^{\prime}}^{t-t^{\prime}+\varepsilon}\int_{s=t^{\prime}-\varepsilon}^{t-\tau}\left|{K_{\alpha^{\prime},\alpha,\sigma}(\tau)}\right|\gamma_{\bm{\zeta}+2\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{R}}_{[t^{\prime}-\varepsilon,s+\tau]},\mathcal{B}}^{X,\mathcal{S}_{\alpha^{\prime}}}(t,s+\tau)dsd\tau.

Figure 6:
The integration region for the double integral
t^{\prime}-\varepsilon\leq s\leq t^{\prime},t^{\prime}-s\leq\tau\leq t-s
is shown in blue. The integral of a non-negative function over this region is upper bounded by the sum of integrals of this function in the two regions shown on the right.

Now, using lemma
9
with
\beta_{0}=2
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}}\int_{\tau=0}^{t-t^{\prime}}\int_{s=t^{\prime}-\varepsilon}^{t^{\prime}}\left|{K_{\alpha^{\prime},\alpha,\sigma}(\tau)}\right|\gamma_{\bm{\zeta}+2\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{R}}_{[t^{\prime}-\varepsilon,s+\tau]},\mathcal{B}}^{X,\mathcal{S}_{\alpha^{\prime}}}(t,s+\tau)dsd\tau

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\leq\varepsilon\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}}\int_{\tau=0}^{t-t^{\prime}}\left|{K_{\alpha^{\prime},\alpha,\sigma}(\tau)}\right|\gamma^{X,\mathcal{S}_{\alpha^{\prime}}}_{\bm{\zeta}+2\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{R}}_{[t^{\prime}-\varepsilon,t^{\prime}+\tau]},\mathcal{B}}(t,t^{\prime}+\tau)d\tau+C\varepsilon^{2}\left\|{\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{L}}(0,t)}\right\|_{\infty},

where
C
is the constant defined in lemma
9
that depends only on
\left|{\mathcal{B}}\right|,\|{\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{L}}(0,t)}\|_{\infty},\|{\bm{\zeta}_{[0,t]}}\|_{\infty}
and
\|{\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{R}}(-t,t)}\|_{\infty}
.
Furthermore, since
\gamma^{X,Y}_{\zeta,\mathcal{B}}(t,t^{\prime})\leq 1
, we also have that

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}}\int_{\tau=t-t^{\prime}}^{t-t^{\prime}+\varepsilon}\int_{s=t^{\prime}-\varepsilon}^{t-\tau}\left|{K_{\alpha^{\prime},\alpha,\sigma}(\tau)}\right|\gamma^{X,\mathcal{S}_{\alpha^{\prime}}}_{\bm{\zeta}+2\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{R}}_{[t^{\prime}-\varepsilon,s+\tau]},\mathcal{B}}(t,s+\tau)ds\ d\tau\leq\varepsilon\int_{t-t^{\prime}}^{t-t^{\prime}+\varepsilon}\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}}\left|{K_{\alpha^{\prime},\alpha,\sigma}(\tau)}\right|d\tau\leq\varepsilon^{2}\left|{\mathcal{B}}\right|\|{K_{[0,t]}}\|_{\infty}.

(87)

Consequently, we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}}\int_{t^{\prime}-\varepsilon}^{t^{\prime}}\int_{t^{\prime}}^{t}\left|{K_{\alpha^{\prime},\alpha,\sigma}(s^{\prime}-s)}\right|\gamma_{\bm{\zeta}+2\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{R}}_{[t^{\prime}-\varepsilon,s^{\prime}]},\mathcal{B}}^{X,\mathcal{S}_{\alpha^{\prime}}}(t,s^{\prime})ds^{\prime}ds

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\leq\varepsilon\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}}\int_{\tau=0}^{t-t^{\prime}}\left|{K_{\alpha^{\prime},\alpha,\sigma}(\tau)}\right|\gamma_{\bm{\zeta}+2\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{R}}_{[t^{\prime}-\varepsilon,t^{\prime}+\tau]},\mathcal{B}}^{X,\mathcal{S}_{\alpha^{\prime}}}(t,t^{\prime}+\tau)d\tau+(C\|{\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{L}}(0,t)}\|_{\infty}+\left|{\mathcal{B}}\right|\|{K_{[0,t]}}\|_{\infty})\varepsilon^{2}.

(88)

From Eqs.
83
,
84
,
85
and
88
, we then obtain that

T_{\varepsilon}^{(1)}(t,t^{\prime})\leq\sum_{\alpha:\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}\cap Y\neq\emptyset}\bigg(2(1+2\left\|{\bm{\zeta}_{[t^{\prime}-\varepsilon,t^{\prime}]}}\right\|_{\infty})\gamma^{X,\mathcal{S}_{\alpha^{\prime}}}_{\bm{\zeta}+2\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{R}}_{[t^{\prime}-\varepsilon,t^{\prime}]},\mathcal{B}}(t,t^{\prime})+2\sum_{\alpha^{\prime},\sigma}\int_{t^{\prime}}^{t}\left|{K_{\alpha^{\prime},\alpha,\sigma}(s^{\prime}-t^{\prime})}\right|\gamma^{X,\mathcal{S}_{\alpha^{\prime}}}_{\bm{\zeta}+2\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{R}}_{[t^{\prime}-\varepsilon,s^{\prime}]},\mathcal{B}}(t,s^{\prime})ds^{\prime}\bigg)+c_{0}\varepsilon^{2},

where
c_{0}=2C\|{\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{L}}(0,t)}\|_{\infty}+10\left|{\mathcal{B}}\right|\|{K_{[0,t]}}\|_{\infty}
. This proves the lemma statement.
∎

X
Lieb-Robinson bounds for independent-bath model

We now apply the common framework to the case where the environment is composed of independent baths. Recall from the main text that, in this model, the index
\alpha
labeling the system-environment interaction operators has the form
\alpha=(i,\nu)
where
\nu\in\{1,2\dots L\}
. Thus, the environment operators are indexed as
B_{\alpha}(t)\cong B_{i}^{(\nu)}(t)
, where the index
i
labels the bath corresponding to
\alpha
and
\nu
labels different possible bath operators within the
i^{\text{th}}
bath. The independence of the different baths is equivalent to assuming that

[B_{i}^{(\nu)}(t),B_{i^{\prime}}^{(\nu^{\prime})}(t^{\prime})]=0\text{ for }i\neq i^{\prime},

and assuming that the initial environment state is a product state on the different baths i.e.
\rho_{E}=\otimes_{i}\rho_{E,i}
where
\rho_{E,i}
is a state of the
i^{\text{th}}
bath. Therefore, the kernels are uncorrelated between different baths i.e.,

K_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}(t)\cong K_{i,\nu,\nu^{\prime}}(t)\delta_{i,i^{\prime}}.

Finally, we will also assume that, given
\alpha=(i,\nu)
, the support
\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}
depends only
i
i.e.
\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}\cong\mathcal{S}_{i}
or, in words, the
i^{\text{th}}
bath only interacts with qubits in the geometrically local region
S_{i}
.

For the rest of this section, it will be convenient to use
m(\tau)
defined in the main text which we recap below: Suppose the kernels
K_{i,\nu,\nu^{\prime}}(t)
have representation given by

K_{i,\nu,\nu^{\prime}}(t)=K_{i,\nu,\nu^{\prime};c}(t)+\sum_{j}k_{i,\nu,\nu^{\prime}}[j]\delta(t-T_{j}),

where
K_{{i,\nu,\nu^{\prime}};c}
is a continuous function and
k_{i,\nu,\nu^{\prime}}\in\mathbb{C}
. Then, for
\tau>0
, define
m(\tau)
via

\displaystyle m(\tau)=\sup_{i,\nu,\nu^{\prime}}\text{TV}(K_{i,\nu^{\prime},\nu};[-\tau,\tau])=\sup_{i,\nu,\nu^{\prime}}\bigg[\int_{-\tau}^{\tau}|{K_{{i,\nu,\nu^{\prime}};c}(\tau)}|d\tau+\sum_{j:T_{j}\in[-\tau,\tau]}|{k_{i,\nu,\nu^{\prime}}[j]}|\bigg]

(89)

We also note that
m(\tau)
is a function which is right-continuous i.e.,

\displaystyle\lim_{\delta\to 0^{+}}m(\tau+\delta)=\sup_{i,\nu,\nu^{\prime}}\lim_{\delta\to 0^{+}}\bigg[\int_{-(\tau+\delta)}^{\tau+\delta}|{K_{i,\nu,\nu^{\prime};c}(\tau)}|d\tau+\sum_{j:T_{j}\in[-(\tau+\delta),\tau+\delta]}|{k_{i,\nu,\nu^{\prime};c}[j]}|\bigg]=m(\tau),

(90)

where we can swap the limit
\delta\to 0
and the supremum over
i,\nu,\nu^{\prime}
since the supremum is only over a finite set.

Importantly, we remind the reader that the framework developed in the previous section (Section
IX
), including the definitions of
\bm{\mu}^{\text{L}/\text{R}}(a,b),\gamma^{X,Y}_{\zeta,\mathcal{B}}(t,t^{\prime})
and associated lemmas, are all for a
\delta-
mollified model i.e., for a model with the kernels

K_{i,\nu,\nu^{\prime}}^{\delta}=(\eta_{\delta}\star\eta_{\delta})\star K_{i,\nu,\nu^{\prime}},

with
\eta_{\delta}
being the mollifier defined in Section
VII.3
. This has to be accounted in the analysis of this section towards proving proposition 1, so we will re-instate indexing by
\delta
in the following proofs.

First, we develop some useful upper bounds on
\|{\mu^{\text{L}}(0,t)}\|_{\infty}
,
\|{\mu^{\text{R}}(0,t)}\|_{\infty}
by specializing the general definitions provided in Section
IX
.

Lemma 12
.

For small enough
\delta
and
\tau\geq 0
,
\mu_{\alpha}^{\textnormal{R}}(0,\tau),\mu_{\alpha}^{\textnormal{R}}(-\tau,\tau)
,
\mu_{\alpha,\sigma}^{\textnormal{L}}(0,\tau)
and
\mu_{\alpha,\sigma}^{\textnormal{L}}(-\tau,\tau)
as defined in Eq. (
67
) and Eq. (
68
) with the
\delta-
mollified kernels satisfy

\mu_{\alpha}^{\textnormal{R}}(-\tau,\tau)\leq\mu_{\alpha}^{\textnormal{R}}(-\tau,\tau)\leq 2Lm(\tau+\delta)\quad\text{and}\quad\mu_{\alpha,\sigma}^{\textnormal{L}}(-\tau,\tau)\leq\mu_{\alpha,\sigma}^{\textnormal{L}}(-\tau,\tau)\leq Lm(\tau+\delta).

Proof.

Suppose
\alpha=(i,\nu)
, then from Eq. (
67
) and lemma
2
, it follows that

\displaystyle\mu_{(i,\nu)}^{\textnormal{R}}(-\tau,\tau)\leq\sum_{\nu^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}}\int_{-\tau}^{\tau}|{K^{\delta}_{i,\nu,\nu^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}}(s)}|ds\leq\sum_{\nu^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}}\textnormal{TV}(K_{i,\sigma^{\prime}}^{(\nu,\nu^{\prime})},[-(\tau+\delta),(\tau+\delta)])\leq 2Lm(\tau+\delta).

(91)

Similarly,

\displaystyle\mu_{(i,\nu),\sigma}^{\textnormal{L}}(-\tau,\tau)\leq\sum_{\nu^{\prime}}\int_{-\tau}^{\tau}|{K^{\delta}_{i,\nu^{\prime},\nu,\sigma}(s)}|ds\leq\sum_{\nu^{\prime}}\textnormal{TV}(K_{i,\nu^{\prime},\nu,\sigma}^{\delta},[-(\tau+\delta),\tau+\delta])\leq Lm(\tau+\delta).

(92)

Finally, noting that
\mu_{\alpha}^{\text{R}}(0,\tau)\leq\mu_{\alpha}^{\text{R}}(-\tau,\tau)
and
\mu_{\alpha,\sigma}^{\text{L}}(0,\tau)\leq\mu_{\alpha,\sigma}^{\text{R}}(-\tau,\tau)
, we obtain the bounds in the lemma statement.
∎

We now prove a Lieb-Robinson bound on
\gamma^{X,Y}_{\zeta,B}(t,t^{\prime})
by building on lemma
11
. Again, the reader can be reminded that
\gamma^{X,Y}_{\zeta,B}(t,t^{\prime})
has also been defined for the
\delta-
mollified kernel (see section
IX
).

Lemma 13
.

For
0\leq t^{\prime}<t
,

\gamma^{X,Y}_{\bm{\zeta},\mathcal{B}}(t,t^{\prime})\leq\left|{Y}\right|\bigg(\exp\bigg(\frac{v_{\textnormal{LR}}(\bm{\zeta},t)\left|{t-t^{\prime}}\right|}{a_{0}}\bigg)-1\bigg)\exp\bigg(-\frac{d_{X,Y}}{a_{0}}\bigg),

where

v_{\textnormal{LR}}(\bm{\zeta},t)=ea_{0}\mathcal{Z}(L+2L\left\|{\bm{\zeta}_{[0,t]}}\right\|_{\infty}+10L^{2}m(t+\delta)),

with
\mathcal{Z},a_{0}
being defined in Eq. (
17
) and
m_{\delta}(\tau)
being defined in Eq. (
89
)

Proof.

Given
\left\|{\bm{\zeta}_{[0,t]}}\right\|_{\infty}<\infty
,
0<t^{\prime}<t
and for
\tau\in(0,t-t^{\prime})
, consider
\Gamma_{X,Y}(\tau)
defined by

\Gamma_{X,Y}(\tau)=\gamma^{X,Y}_{\bm{\zeta}+2\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{R}}_{[t^{\prime},t-\tau]},\mathcal{B}}(t,t-\tau).

From lemma
11
, we then obtain that for sufficiently small
\varepsilon

\displaystyle\Gamma_{X,Y}(\tau+\varepsilon)-\Gamma_{X,Y}(\tau)

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\sum_{\alpha:\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}\cap Y\neq\emptyset}\bigg(2\bigg(1+2\left\|{\bm{\zeta}_{[0,t]}}\right\|_{\infty}+4\sup_{s\in[t-\tau-\varepsilon,t-\tau]}\left\|{\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{R}}_{[t^{\prime},t-\tau-\varepsilon]}(s)}\right\|_{\infty}\bigg)\Gamma_{X,\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}}(\tau)+2\sum_{\alpha^{\prime},\sigma}\int_{t-\tau}^{t}|{K^{\delta}_{\alpha^{\prime},\alpha,\sigma}(s^{\prime}-t+\tau)}|\Gamma_{X,\mathcal{S}_{\alpha^{\prime}}}(t-s^{\prime})ds^{\prime}\bigg)+C_{1}\varepsilon^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\sum_{\alpha:\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}\cap Y\neq\emptyset}\bigg(2\bigg(1+2\left\|{\bm{\zeta}_{[0,t]}}\right\|_{\infty}+4\left\|{\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{R}}(0,t)}\right\|_{\infty}\bigg)\Gamma_{X,\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}}(\tau)+2\underbrace{\sum_{\alpha^{\prime},\sigma}\int_{0}^{\tau}|{K^{\delta}_{\alpha^{\prime},\alpha,\sigma}(\tau-\tau^{\prime})}|\Gamma_{X,\mathcal{S}_{\alpha^{\prime}}}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}}_{\Lambda_{X,\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}}(\tau)}\bigg)+C_{1}\varepsilon^{2}.

(93)

This inequality can be converted into its integral form. More explicitly, for a given
\tau
we choose
\varepsilon=\tau/N
and define
\tau_{n}=n\varepsilon
. Then, from Eq.
93
, we obtain

\Gamma_{X,Y}(\tau_{n})\leq\Gamma_{X,Y}(\tau_{n-1})+\varepsilon\bigg(\sum_{\alpha:\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}\cap Y\neq\emptyset}2\bigg(1+2\left\|{\bm{\zeta}_{[0,t]}}\right\|_{\infty}+4\left\|{\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{R}}(0,t)}\right\|_{\infty}\bigg)\Gamma_{X,\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}}(\tau_{n-1})+2\Lambda_{X,\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}}(\tau_{n-1})\bigg)+\frac{C_{1}\tau^{2}}{N^{2}},

and consequently

\Gamma_{X,Y}(\tau_{N})\leq\Gamma_{X,Y}(\tau_{0})+\sum_{\alpha:\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}\cap Y\neq\emptyset}\bigg[2\bigg(1+2\left\|{\bm{\zeta}_{[0,t]}}\right\|_{\infty}+4\left\|{\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{R}}(0,t)}\right\|_{\infty}\bigg)\bigg(\frac{\tau}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\Gamma_{X,\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}}(\tau_{n})\bigg)+2\bigg(\frac{\tau}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\Lambda_{X,\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}}(\tau_{n})\bigg)\bigg]+\frac{C_{1}\tau^{2}}{N}.

Taking the limt of
N\to\infty
in this inequality, and the fact that from lemma
32
a and b it follows that
\Gamma_{X,\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}}
and
\Lambda_{X,\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}}
are both integrable functions, we obtain that

\displaystyle\Gamma_{X,Y}(\tau)

\displaystyle\leq\Gamma_{X,Y}(0)+\sum_{\alpha:\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}\cap Y\neq\emptyset}\bigg[2\big(1+2\left\|{\bm{\zeta}_{[0,t]}}\right\|_{\infty}+4\left\|{\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{R}}(0,t)}\right\|_{\infty}\big)\int_{0}^{\tau}\Gamma_{X,\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}+2\int_{0}^{\tau}\Lambda_{X,\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}\bigg]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle(\mkern-1.5mu1\mkern-1.5mu)}}{{\leq}}\Gamma_{X,Y}(0)+L\sum_{i:\mathcal{S}_{i}\cap Y\neq\emptyset}\bigg[2\big(1+2\left\|{\bm{\zeta}_{[0,t]}}\right\|_{\infty}+4\left\|{\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{R}}(0,t)}\right\|_{\infty}\big)\int_{0}^{\tau}\Gamma_{X,\mathcal{S}_{i}}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}+2\int_{0}^{\tau}\Lambda_{X,\mathcal{S}_{i}}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}\bigg],

(94)

where in (1) we have used the fact that, for
\alpha=(i,\nu)
,
\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}=\mathcal{S}_{i}
. Next, we observe that, if
\alpha=(i,\nu)
and
\alpha^{\prime}=(i^{\prime},\nu^{\prime})
then
K_{\alpha^{\prime},\alpha,\sigma}(s)=\delta_{i,i^{\prime}}K_{i}^{(\nu,\nu^{\prime})}(s)
. Together with the fact that
\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}
depends only on
i
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\tau}\Lambda_{X,\mathcal{S}_{i}}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\tau}\Lambda_{X,\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\alpha^{\prime},\sigma}\int_{\tau^{\prime}=0}^{\tau}\int_{\tau^{\prime\prime}=0}^{\tau^{\prime}}\left|{K^{\delta}_{\alpha^{\prime},\alpha,\sigma}(\tau^{\prime}-\tau^{\prime\prime})}\right|\Gamma_{X,\mathcal{S}_{\alpha^{\prime}}}(\tau^{\prime\prime})d\tau^{\prime\prime}d\tau^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i^{\prime},\nu^{\prime},\sigma}\int_{\tau^{\prime}=0}^{\tau}\int_{\tau^{\prime\prime}=0}^{\tau^{\prime}}\left|{K_{i,\nu,\nu^{\prime},\sigma}^{\delta}(\tau^{\prime}-\tau^{\prime\prime})}\right|\Gamma_{X,\mathcal{S}_{i^{\prime}}}(\tau^{\prime\prime})\delta_{i,i^{\prime}}d\tau^{\prime\prime}d\tau^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\int_{\tau^{\prime\prime}=0}^{\tau}\bigg[\sum_{\nu^{\prime},\sigma}\int_{\tau^{\prime}=\tau^{\prime\prime}}^{\tau}\left|{K_{i,\nu,\nu^{\prime},\sigma}^{\delta}(\tau^{\prime}-\tau^{\prime\prime})}\right|\bigg]\Gamma_{X,\mathcal{S}_{i}}(\tau^{\prime\prime})d\tau^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle\leq 2\left\|{\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{L}}(0,t)}\right\|_{\infty}\int_{0}^{\tau}\Gamma_{X,\mathcal{S}_{i}}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}.

(95)

From Eqs. (
94
) and (
95
), we obtain that

\displaystyle\Gamma_{X,Y}(\tau)\leq

\displaystyle\Gamma_{X,Y}(0)+\omega_{0}\sum_{{\alpha}:\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}\cap Y\neq\emptyset}\int_{0}^{\tau}\Gamma_{X,S_{i}}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime},

(96)

where

\omega_{0}=2\big(L+2L\left\|{\bm{\zeta}_{[0,t]}}\right\|_{\infty}+4L\left\|{\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{R}}(0,t)}\right\|_{\infty}+2L\left\|{\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{L}}(0,t)}\right\|_{\infty}\big).

From lemma
12
it then follows that
\omega_{0}=2(L+2L\|{\bm{\zeta}_{[0,t]}}\|_{\infty}+10L^{2}m(t+\delta))
.
Now, applying lemma
4
and setting
\tau=t-t^{\prime}
, we obtain the lemma statement.
∎

Proof of proposition 1 from main text.

Let us first analyze
\Delta_{O_{X}}(t,t^{\prime};l)
, as defined in the main text, for the
\delta-
mollified model. We will make use of lemma
5
: Note that, for the independent bath model,
\Delta_{O_{X}}^{\text{L}}(t,t^{\prime};l)
, defined in lemma
5
, is 0 since if, for any set
Y
,
\alpha\in\mathcal{A}_{Y}=\{\alpha:\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}\cap Y=\emptyset\}
and
\alpha^{\prime}\notin\mathcal{A}_{Y}
, then
\alpha
and
\alpha^{\prime}
correspond to different baths and thus
K_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}=0
. Hence, the total truncation error
\Delta_{O_{X}}(t,t^{\prime};l)
equals the right-contraction contribution
\Delta_{O_{X}}^{\text{R}}(t,t^{\prime};l)
bounded in lemma
8
via

\displaystyle\Delta_{O_{X}}(t,t^{\prime};l)=\Delta_{O_{X}}^{\text{R}}(t,t^{\prime};l)\leq 2\left\|{O_{X}}\right\|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\notin\mathcal{A}_{X[l]}\\
\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}\cap X_{c}[l]\neq\emptyset\end{subarray}}\left(1+\mu^{\textnormal{R}}_{\alpha}(0,t)\right)\int_{s=t^{\prime}}^{t}\gamma^{X,\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}}_{\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{R}}_{[0,s]},\mathcal{A}_{X[l]}}(t,s)ds,

(97)

Noting the fact that if
\bm{\zeta}=\bm{\mu}^{\text{R}}_{[0,s]}
for
0\leq s\leq t
, then

\displaystyle\|{\bm{\zeta}_{[0,t]}}\|_{\infty}=\max_{\alpha}\sup_{s^{\prime}\in[0,t]}\mu^{\text{R}}_{\alpha,[0,s]}(s^{\prime})=\max_{\alpha}\sup_{s^{\prime}\in[0,t]}\mu^{\text{R}}_{\alpha}(s^{\prime}-s,s^{\prime})\leq\max_{\alpha}\mu^{\text{R}}_{\alpha}(-t,t)\leq 2Lm(t+\delta),

where in the last step we have used lemma
12
. With this estimate together with lemma
13
, it follows that

\gamma^{X,\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}}_{\bm{\mu}^{\text{R}}_{[0,s]},\mathcal{A}_{X[l]}}(t,s)\leq\left|{\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}}\right|\exp\bigg(\frac{v_{\text{LR}}(t)|t-s|}{a_{0}}\bigg)\exp\bigg(-\frac{d_{X,\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}}}{a_{0}}\bigg),

where

v_{\text{LR}}(t)=ea_{0}\mathcal{Z}L(1+14Lm(t+\delta)).

Returning to Eq. (
97
) and using lemma
12
to upper bound
\mu_{\alpha}^{\text{R}}(0,t)
by
2Lm(t+\delta)

\Delta_{O_{X}}(t,t^{\prime};l)\leq 2\left\|{O_{X}}\right\|(1+2Lm_{\delta}(t))\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\notin\mathcal{A}_{X[l]}\\
\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}\cap X^{c}[l]\neq\emptyset\end{subarray}}\left|{\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}}\right|\int_{s=0}^{t-t^{\prime}}\exp\bigg(\frac{v_{\text{LR}}(t)s-d_{X,\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}}}{a_{0}}\bigg)ds.

Now, we note that by definition of
\mathcal{A}_{X[l]}
(lemma
5
), for any
\alpha\notin\mathcal{A}_{X[l]}
,
d_{X,\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}}\geq l
. Furthermore, in
d
dimensions and given the fact that
\text{diam}(\mathcal{S}_{\alpha})\leq a_{0}
,
\left|{\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}}\right|\leq(a_{0}+1)^{d}
. Therefore,

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\notin\mathcal{A}_{X[l]}\\
\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}\cap X^{c}[l]\neq\emptyset\end{subarray}}\left|{\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}}\right|\int_{s=0}^{t-t^{\prime}}\exp\bigg(\frac{v_{\text{LR}}(t)s-d_{X,\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}}}{a_{0}}\bigg)ds\leq\frac{a_{0}(a_{0}+1)^{d}}{v_{\text{LR}}(t)}\exp\bigg(-\frac{l}{a_{0}}\bigg)\bigg(\exp\bigg(\frac{v_{\text{LR}}(t)\left|{t-t^{\prime}}\right|}{a_{0}}\bigg)-1\bigg)\bigg(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\notin\mathcal{A}_{X[l]}\\
\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}\cap X^{c}[l]\neq\emptyset\end{subarray}}1\bigg).

Now, note that the number of lattice sites in the set
X^{c}[l]\setminus X[l]
is
(\text{diam}(X)+2l+a_{0})^{d}-(\text{diam}(X)+2l)^{d}
and since any one lattice site can be in at-most
\mathcal{Z}
sub-regions
\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}
, we obtain that

\left|{\{\alpha:\alpha\notin\mathcal{A}_{X[l]}\text{ and }\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}\cap X^{c}[l]\neq\emptyset\}}\right|\leq\mathcal{Z}\big[(\text{diam}(X)+2l+a_{0})^{d}-(\text{diam}(X)+2l)^{d}\big].

Together with the bound
v_{\text{LR}}(t)\geq ea_{0}\mathcal{Z}L(1+2Lm_{\delta}(t))
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\Delta_{O_{X}}(t,t^{\prime};l)\leq\left\|{O_{X}}\right\|\frac{2(a_{0}+1)^{d}}{eL}\big[(\text{diam}(X)+2l+a_{0})^{d}-(\text{diam}(X)+2l)^{d}\big]\exp\bigg(-\frac{l}{a_{0}}\bigg)\bigg(\exp\bigg(\frac{v_{\text{LR}}(t)\left|{t-t^{\prime}}\right|}{a_{0}}\bigg)-1\bigg).

(98)

Note that
\big[(\text{diam}(X)+2l+a_{0})^{d}-(\text{diam}(X)+2l)^{d}\big]\leq O(l^{d-1})
for large
l
.

Finally, we remark that, thus far, we have analyze
\Delta_{O_{X}}(t,t^{\prime};l)
for
\delta-
mollified model. However, as was established in Proposition
4
,
\Delta_{O_{X}}(t,t^{\prime};l)
converges to its value for the actual model as
\delta\to 0
. Thus, taking the limit of
\delta\to 0^{+}
in Eq. (
98
), which entails replacing
m(t+\delta)
by
m(t)
(due to the right-continuity of
m
) on the right-hand side in
v_{\text{LR}}(t)
proves the proposition.
∎

This completes the independent-bath proof of Proposition 1. The following refinement remains within the independent-bath setting and improves the estimate of the Lieb-Robinson velocity. Consider the setting where the some of the baths only couple to a single site in the system, then physically it is expected that the Lieb-Robinson velocity in the model is independent of the memory kernel of these baths. To see how this can be proved, define the Hamiltonian

\tilde{H}_{\text{SE}}(t)=\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\sum_{\nu=1}^{L}R_{i}^{(\nu)}(t)B_{i}^{(\nu)}(t),

where
\mathcal{I}
indexes the set of baths which couple only to a single site in the system i.e., for each
i\in\mathcal{I}
,
R_{i}^{(1)}(t),R_{i}^{(2)}(t)\dots R_{i}^{(\nu)}(t)
are supported on the same single site. In this case, to provide a Lieb-Robinson bound, we simply move into the interaction picture with respect to
\tilde{H}_{\text{SE}}(t)
: This then yields the Hamiltonian

\tilde{H}(t)=\sum_{\alpha}\tilde{h}_{\alpha}(t)+\sum_{i\notin\mathcal{I}}\sum_{\nu=1}^{L}\tilde{R}^{(\nu)}_{i}(t)B_{i}^{(\nu)}(t),

where

\displaystyle\tilde{R}^{(\nu)}_{i}(t)=\tilde{U}(0,t)R_{i}^{(\nu)}(t)\tilde{U}(t,0)\quad\text{and}\quad\tilde{h}_{\alpha}(t)=\tilde{U}(0,t)h_{\alpha}(t)\tilde{U}(t,0),

(99)

where
\tilde{U}(t,s)
is the unitary generated by
\tilde{H}_{\text{SE}}(t)
defined above. Since
\tilde{H}_{\text{SE}}(t)
only contains terms supported on single system sites, the operators
\tilde{R}^{(\nu)}_{i}(t)
and
\tilde{h}_{\alpha}(t)
have the same support on the system sites as
{R}^{(\nu)}_{i}(t)
and
{h}_{\alpha}(t)
. Furthermore, the bath operators appearing in the rotated Hamiltonian
\tilde{H}(t)
still commute with
\tilde{R}^{(\nu)}_{i}(t)
and
\tilde{h}_{\alpha}(t)
simply because they correspond to different baths than the ones in
\tilde{H}_{\text{SE}}(t)
. Consequently, the analysis in done thus far can instead be performed with
\tilde{H}(t)
and would yield a Lieb-Robinson bound as in proposition 1 but with a modified
m(\tau)
given below

m(\tau)=\sup_{i\notin\mathcal{I},\nu,\nu^{\prime}}\text{TV}(K_{i,\nu,\nu^{\prime}};[-\tau,\tau])\leq\sup_{i,\nu,\nu^{\prime}}\text{TV}(K_{i,\nu,\nu^{\prime}};[-\tau,\tau]).

Thus, moving into interaction picture with respect to the system-environment terms corresponding to baths only coupling with single system sites allows us to improve the upper bound on the Lieb-Robinson velocity derived in proposition 1.

XI
Case of coupled bath

We now refer to the common framework developed in the previous section, but no longer assume that different bath supports are independent. Instead, we assume that the environment itself has a light cone,
i.e., the kernels
K_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime},\sigma}
are small when
\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}

and
\mathcal{S}_{\alpha^{\prime}}
are very far apart. To formalize this notion, we
associate each environmental operator
B_{\alpha}(t)
with a distinct
position
\text{x}_{\alpha}
in the environment. We require the distances between the points
\text{x}_{\alpha}
to be commensurate with the lattice
distance between the regions
\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}
i.e., the farther apart
the regions
\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}
and
\mathcal{S}_{\beta}
on the lattice the farther
apart should the points
\text{x}_{\alpha}
and
\text{x}_{\beta}
be. We formalize this by requiring the existence of constants
p_{\pm},q_{\pm}>0
such that

d(\mathcal{S}_{\alpha},\mathcal{S}_{\beta})\leq p_{+}d(\text{x}_{\alpha},\text{x}_{\beta})+q_{+}\text{ and }d(\text{x}_{\alpha},\text{x}_{\beta})\leq p_{-}d(\mathcal{S}_{\alpha},\mathcal{S}_{\beta})+q_{-}.

(100)

We further require that
d(\text{x}_{\alpha},\text{x}_{\beta})
satisfies the triangle inequality, whereas
d(S_{\alpha},S_{\beta})
(being a distance between two sets) may not.

We will now impose the following condition on the kernels
K_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime},\sigma}(s)

to capture a finite velocity of propagation in the environment.

Assumption 2

(Light-cone in the environment)
.

Suppose the kernels
K_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}(\tau)
have the representation

K_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}(\tau)=K_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime};c}(\tau)+\sum_{j}k_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}[j]\delta(\tau-T_{j}).

We assume that there exists a non-negative constants
K_{0},a_{E},\lambda_{E}>0
such that for all
\tau>0

\textnormal{TV}(K_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}};[-\tau,\tau])=\int_{-\tau}^{\tau}|K_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime};c}(s)|ds+\sum_{T_{j}\in[-\tau,\tau]}|{k_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}[j]}|\leq K_{0}\min\big({\exp\left(\lambda_{E}|\tau|-d(\textnormal{x}_{\alpha},\textnormal{x}_{\alpha^{\prime}})/a_{E}\right)},1\big)

(101)

where
d(\textnormal{x}_{\alpha},\textnormal{x}_{\alpha^{\prime}})
is defined in Eq. (
100
).

In the limit of
a_{E}\to 0

and
\lambda_{E}\to 0
and assuming that
\textnormal{x}_{\alpha}\neq\textnormal{x}_{\alpha^{\prime}}
if
\alpha\neq\alpha^{\prime}
, we obtain an independent bath model i.e.,
K_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}=0
unless
\alpha=\alpha^{\prime}
. More generally, the parameter
a_{E}\lambda_{E}
can be interpreted as the propagation velocity of excitations in the environment, and when non-zero, it can modify the speed at which information propagates in the system.
We first provide a lemma which replaces the upper bound in assumption
2
by a different upper-bound which is more convenient to handle in the following Lieb-Robinson proof.

Lemma 14
.

Suppose the kernels
K_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}
satisfy assumption
2
. For any fixed
0<\kappa_{E}<1/a_{E}
, there exist
\eta>d
(where
d
is the lattice dimension) and
0<\lambda_{0}<\min(1/q_{-},1/q_{+})
(where
q_{\pm}
are introduced in Eq. (
100
)) such that for
\tau>0

\displaystyle\textnormal{TV}(K_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}};[-\tau,\tau])=\int_{-\tau}^{\tau}|K_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime};c}(s)|ds+\sum_{T_{j}\in[-\tau,\tau]}|{k_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}[j]}|\leq K_{0}\min\bigg(\frac{\exp(\lambda_{E}\left|{\tau}\right|-\kappa_{E}d(\textnormal{x}_{\alpha},\textnormal{x}_{\alpha^{\prime}}))}{(1+\lambda_{0}d(\textnormal{x}_{\alpha},\textnormal{x}_{\alpha^{\prime}}))^{\eta}},1\bigg)

(102)

Proof.

We begin by picking
\kappa_{E}
as any constant such that
\kappa_{E}<1/a_{E}
. Defining
\epsilon=1/a_{E}-\kappa_{E}
, it then follows that for any
\eta>0

\exp(-d(\text{x}_{\alpha},\text{x}_{\alpha^{\prime}})/a_{E})=\exp(-\kappa_{E}d(\text{x}_{\alpha},\text{x}_{\alpha^{\prime}}))\exp(-\epsilon d(\text{x}_{\alpha},\text{x}_{\alpha^{\prime}}))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle(\mkern-1.5mu1\mkern-1.5mu)}}{{\leq}}\frac{\exp(-\kappa_{E}d(\text{x}_{\alpha},\text{x}_{\alpha^{\prime}}))}{(1+\epsilon d(\text{x}_{\alpha},\text{x}_{\alpha^{\prime}})/\eta)^{\eta}},

where in (1) we have used the inequality
(1+x)^{a}\leq e^{ax}\ \forall\ a,x\geq 0
. Using the fact that if
A\leq B
, then
\min(A,1)\leq\min(B,1)
, we then obtain

\int_{-\tau}^{\tau}|K_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime};c}(s)|ds+\sum_{T_{j}\in[-\tau,\tau]}|{k_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}[j]}|\leq K_{0}\min\bigg(\frac{\exp(\lambda_{E}\left|{\tau}\right|-\kappa_{E}d(\textnormal{x}_{\alpha},\textnormal{x}_{\alpha^{\prime}}))}{(1+\lambda_{0}d(\textnormal{x}_{\alpha},\textnormal{x}_{\alpha^{\prime}}))^{\eta}},1\bigg),

where
\lambda_{0}=\epsilon/\eta
. Finally, note that thus far
\eta
is a free parameter: We pick
\eta>\max(d,\epsilon q_{-},\epsilon q_{+})
to satisfy the constraints in the lemma statement.
∎

We also provide a technical lemmas that will be useful in the following analysis. The proof of this lemma is provided in the appendix
D
.

Lemma 15
.

The following summations inequalities are true

(a)

\exists D_{0},D_{\partial 0}>0
and
D_{\partial 1}\geq 0
dependent only on the lattice dimension
d
and the constants
a_{0},\mathcal{Z},p_{\pm},q_{\pm}
such that for all
\alpha
and
l\geq 1
,

\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}:d(\textnormal{x}_{\alpha},\textnormal{x}_{\alpha^{\prime}})\leq l}1\leq D_{0}l^{d}\quad\textnormal{and}\quad\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}:l\leq d(\textnormal{x}_{\alpha},\textnormal{x}_{\alpha^{\prime}})<l+1}1\leq D_{\partial 0}l^{d-1}+D_{\partial 1}l^{d}.

Furthermore,
D_{\partial 1}=0
if
p_{+}p_{-}=1
.

(b)

For
\eta>d
, where
d
is the dimensionality of the lattice, then
\exists B_{\eta}>0
dependent only on
\eta
,
d
,
\mathcal{Z}
,
a_{0}
,
p_{+}
and
q_{+}
such that for all
\alpha

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}}\frac{1}{(1+d(\textnormal{x}_{\alpha},\textnormal{x}_{\alpha^{\prime}}))^{\eta}}\leq\frac{B_{\eta}}{2^{\eta}}.

(103)

Next, we establish upper bounds on
\mu^{\text{R}}_{\alpha}(-t,t)
and
\mu^{\text{L}}_{\alpha,\sigma}(-t,t)
. We remind the reader that these are defined in section
IX
, and are defined with the
\delta
-mollified kernels.

Lemma 16
.

For
\tau>0
, the quantity
\left\|{\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{R}}(-\tau,\tau)}\right\|_{\infty}
,

\left\|{\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{L}}(-\tau,\tau)}\right\|_{\infty}
are finite for fixed
\tau
and satisfy the upper bounds

\left\|{\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{R}}(-\tau,\tau)}\right\|_{\infty}\leq 2(C_{E}(\tau+2\delta)^{d}+B_{E})\quad\text{and}\quad\left\|{\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{L}}(-\tau,\tau)}\right\|_{\infty}\leq C_{E}(\tau+2\delta)^{d}+B_{E},

(104)

where
C_{E}=K_{0}D_{0}\lambda_{E}^{d}/\kappa_{E}^{d}
and
B_{E}=K_{0}B_{\eta}/(2^{\eta}\min(1,\lambda_{0})^{\eta})
with
D_{0}
being defined in lemma
15
.

Proof.

Recall from lemma
2
that the
\delta-
mollified kernels,
K_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}}^{\delta,\delta}=(\eta_{\delta}\star\eta_{\delta})\star K_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}}
, where
\eta_{\delta}
is defined in section
VII.3
, satisfy

\displaystyle\int_{-\tau}^{\tau}|{K_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}}^{\delta}(s)}|ds

\displaystyle\leq\int_{-(\tau+2\delta)}^{\tau+2\delta}|K_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime};c}(s)|ds+\sum_{T_{j}\in[-(\tau+2\delta),\tau+2\delta]}|{k_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}[j]}|

\displaystyle\leq K_{0}\min\bigg(\frac{\exp(\lambda_{E}(\tau+2\delta)-\kappa_{E}d(\textnormal{x}_{\alpha},\textnormal{x}_{\alpha^{\prime}}))}{(1+\lambda_{0}d(\textnormal{x}_{\alpha},\textnormal{x}_{\alpha^{\prime}}))^{\eta}},1\bigg).

(105)

Since
\mu^{\text{R}}_{\alpha}(-\tau,\tau)
is defined for the mollified kernels, we have that

\displaystyle\left\|{\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{R}}(-\tau,\tau)}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\max_{\alpha}\sum_{\alpha^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}}\int_{-\tau}^{\tau}|{K^{\delta}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}}(s)}|ds,

\displaystyle\leq 2K_{0}\max_{\alpha}\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}}\min\bigg(\frac{\exp(\lambda_{E}(\tau+2\delta)-\kappa_{E}d(\textnormal{x}_{\alpha},\textnormal{x}_{\alpha^{\prime}}))}{(1+\lambda_{0}d(\textnormal{x}_{\alpha},\textnormal{x}_{\alpha^{\prime}}))^{\eta}},1\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq 2K_{0}\max_{\alpha}\bigg(\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}:d(\text{x}_{\alpha},\text{x}_{\alpha^{\prime}})\leq\lambda_{E}(\tau+2\delta)/\kappa_{E}}1+\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}:d(\text{x}_{\alpha},\text{x}_{\alpha^{\prime}})\geq\lambda_{E}(\tau+2\delta)/\kappa_{E}}\frac{\exp(\lambda_{E}(\tau+2\delta)-\kappa_{E}d(\textnormal{x}_{\alpha},\textnormal{x}_{\alpha^{\prime}}))}{(1+\lambda_{0}d(\textnormal{x}_{\alpha},\textnormal{x}_{\alpha^{\prime}}))^{\eta}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq 2K_{0}\max_{\alpha}\bigg(\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}:d(\text{x}_{\alpha},\text{x}_{\alpha^{\prime}})\leq\lambda_{E}(\tau+2\delta)/\kappa_{E}}1+\frac{1}{(\min(1,\lambda_{0}))^{\eta}}\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}:d(\text{x}_{\alpha},\text{x}_{\alpha^{\prime}})\geq\lambda_{E}(\tau+2\delta)/\kappa_{E}}\frac{1}{(1+d(\textnormal{x}_{\alpha},\textnormal{x}_{\alpha^{\prime}}))^{\eta}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle(\mkern-1.5mu1\mkern-1.5mu)}}{{\leq}}2K_{0}\left(\frac{D_{0}\lambda_{E}^{d}}{\kappa_{E}^{d}}(\tau+2\delta)^{d}+\frac{B_{\eta}}{2^{\eta}(\min(1,\lambda_{0}))^{\eta}}\right).

(106)

where in (1) we have used lemma
15
. Substituting
C_{E}=K_{0}D_{0}\lambda_{E}^{d}/\kappa_{E}^{d}
and
B_{E}=K_{0}B_{\eta}/(2^{\eta}\min(1,\lambda_{0})^{\eta})
, we obtain

\left\|{\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{R}}(-\tau,\tau)}\right\|_{\infty}\leq 2(C_{E}(\tau+2\delta)^{d}+B_{E}).

(107)

A similar analysis can be used to derive an upper bound on
\left\|{\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{L}}(-\tau,\tau)}\right\|_{\infty}.

∎

We now start from the same Taylor estimate Lemma
10
, and establish the recursion relation.

Lemma 17
.

Given
0\leq t^{\prime}<t
and
\tau\in(0,t-t^{\prime})
, define

\Gamma_{X,Y}(\tau)=\gamma_{\bm{\zeta}+2\bm{\mu}_{[t^{\prime},t-\tau]}^{R}}^{X,Y}(t,t-\tau)
.
For coupled baths, we derive the following integral inequality:

\Gamma_{X,Y}(\tau)\leq\Gamma_{X,Y}(0)+\omega(t)\sum_{\alpha:S_{\alpha}\cap Y\neq\emptyset}\int_{0}^{\tau}\Gamma_{X,S_{\alpha}}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}+2\sum_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime},\sigma:S_{\alpha}\cap Y\neq\emptyset}\int_{0}^{\tau}\Gamma_{X,S_{\alpha^{\prime}}}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}\int_{0}^{\tau-\tau^{\prime}}\left|{K^{(\delta)}_{\alpha^{\prime},\alpha,\sigma}(\tau^{\prime\prime})}\right|d\tau^{\prime\prime},

(108)

where

\omega(t)=2(1+2\|{\bm{\zeta}_{[0,t]}}\|_{\infty}+4\|{\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{R}}(-t,t)}\|_{\infty}).

(109)

Proof.

From lemma
11
, for sufficiently small
\varepsilon
, we have

\displaystyle\Gamma_{X,Y}(\tau+\varepsilon)-\Gamma_{X,Y}(\tau)

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\sum_{\alpha:S_{\alpha}\cap Y\neq\emptyset}\left(2(1+2\left\|{\bm{\zeta}_{[0,t]}}\right\|_{\infty}+4\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\left\|{\bm{\mu}_{[t^{\prime},t-\tau]}^{R}(s)}\right\|_{\infty})\Gamma_{X,S_{\alpha}}(\tau)+2\sum_{\alpha^{\prime},\sigma}\int_{t-\tau}^{t}\left|{K^{\delta}_{\alpha^{\prime},\alpha,\sigma}(s^{\prime}-t+\tau)}\right|\Gamma_{X,S_{\alpha^{\prime}}}(t-s^{\prime})ds^{\prime}\right)+C_{1}\varepsilon^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\sum_{\alpha:S_{\alpha}\cap Y\neq\emptyset}\left(2(1+2\left\|{\bm{\zeta}_{[0,t]}}\right\|_{\infty}+4\left\|{\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{R}}(-t,t)}\right\|_{\infty})\Gamma_{X,S_{\alpha}}(\tau)+2\underbrace{\sum_{\alpha^{\prime},\sigma}\int_{0}^{\tau}\left|{K^{\delta}_{\alpha^{\prime},\alpha,\sigma}(\tau-\tau^{\prime})}\right|\Gamma_{X,S_{\alpha^{\prime}}}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}}_{\Lambda_{X,S_{\alpha}}(\tau)}\right)+C_{1}\varepsilon^{2}.

(110)

Here, we remind the reader that lemma
11
was derived for the
\delta-
mollified kernel, and therefore we have used
K^{(\delta)}_{\alpha^{\prime},\alpha,\sigma}
in the above inequality. Following steps similar to those in Lemma
13
, we take
\varepsilon=\tau/N
for sufficiently large
N
and arrive at the integral inequality for
\Gamma_{X,Y}(\tau)
:

\displaystyle\Gamma_{X,Y}(\tau)\leq{}

\displaystyle\Gamma_{X,Y}(0)+\sum_{\alpha:S_{\alpha}\cap Y\neq\emptyset}2(1+2\left\|{\bm{\zeta}_{[0,t]}}\right\|_{\infty}+4\left\|{\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{R}}(-t,t)}\right\|_{\infty})\int_{0}^{\tau}\Gamma_{X,S_{\alpha}}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}

(111)

\displaystyle+2\sum_{\alpha:S_{\alpha}\cap Y\neq\emptyset}\int_{0}^{\tau}\Lambda_{X,S_{\alpha}}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}.

Finally, we note that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\tau}\Lambda_{X,S_{\alpha}}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\alpha^{\prime},\sigma}\int_{\tau^{\prime}=0}^{\tau}\int_{\tau^{\prime\prime}=0}^{\tau^{\prime}}\left|{K^{\delta}_{\alpha^{\prime},\alpha,\sigma}(\tau^{\prime}-\tau^{\prime\prime})}\right|\Gamma_{X,S_{\alpha^{\prime}}}(\tau^{\prime\prime})d\tau^{\prime\prime}d\tau^{\prime}

(112)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\alpha^{\prime},\sigma}\int_{\tau^{\prime}=0}^{\tau}\Gamma_{X,S_{\alpha^{\prime}}}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}\int_{0}^{\tau-\tau^{\prime}}\left|{K^{\delta}_{\alpha^{\prime},\alpha,\sigma}(\tau^{\prime\prime})}\right|d\tau^{\prime\prime},

which completes the proof.

∎

Compared with Lemma
13
, the integral inequality derived above exhibits a tail across different system sites, originating from the coupling between distinct baths. To derive the corresponding Lieb–Robinson bound, we thus need to sum over contributions from the entire system. To streamline the remaining proof, we first provide a solution of a modified Lieb-Robinson integral equation, which we will later apply to provide a Lieb-Robinson bound. We begin with the proof of a simple but useful inequality.

Lemma 18
.

For
\tau>0
, define
F_{\alpha,\beta}(\tau)
via

\displaystyle F_{\alpha,\beta}(\tau)=\frac{\exp(\lambda_{E}\tau-\kappa_{E}d(\textnormal{x}_{\alpha},\textnormal{x}_{\beta}))}{(1+d(\textnormal{x}_{\alpha},\textnormal{x}_{\beta}))^{\eta}},

(113)

then for
\tau_{1},\tau_{2}>0

\displaystyle\sum_{\gamma}F_{\alpha,\gamma}(\tau_{1})F_{\gamma,\beta}(\tau_{2})\leq B_{\eta}F_{\alpha,\beta}(\tau_{1}+\tau_{2}),

(114)

where
B_{\eta}
is the constant defined in lemma
15
(b).

Proof.

For
\tau_{1},\tau_{2}>0

\displaystyle\sum_{\gamma}F_{\alpha,\gamma}(\tau_{1})F_{\gamma,\beta}(\tau_{2})

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\gamma}\frac{\exp(\lambda_{E}(\tau_{1}+\tau_{2}))\exp[-\kappa_{E}(d(\text{x}_{\alpha},\text{x}_{\gamma})+d(\text{x}_{\gamma},\text{x}_{\beta}))]}{(1+d(\text{x}_{\alpha},\text{x}_{\gamma}))^{\eta}(1+d(\text{x}_{\gamma},\text{x}_{\beta}))^{\eta}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\gamma}\frac{\exp(\lambda_{E}(\tau_{1}+\tau_{2}))\exp[-\kappa_{E}(d(\text{x}_{\alpha},\text{x}_{\gamma})+d(\text{x}_{\gamma},\text{x}_{\beta}))]}{(1+d(\text{x}_{\alpha},\text{x}_{\beta}))^{\eta}}\frac{(1+d(\text{x}_{\alpha},\text{x}_{\beta}))^{\eta}}{(1+d(\text{x}_{\alpha},\text{x}_{\gamma}))^{\eta}(1+d(\text{x}_{\gamma},\text{x}_{\beta}))^{\eta}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle(\mkern-1.5mu1\mkern-1.5mu)}}{{\leq}}\frac{\exp(\lambda_{E}(\tau_{1}+\tau_{2}))\exp[-\kappa_{E}d(\text{x}_{\alpha},\text{x}_{\beta})]}{(1+d(\text{x}_{\alpha},\text{x}_{\beta}))^{\eta}}\sum_{\gamma}\frac{(1+d(\text{x}_{\alpha},\text{x}_{\gamma})+d(\text{x}_{\gamma},\text{x}_{\beta}))^{\eta}}{(1+d(\text{x}_{\alpha},\text{x}_{\gamma}))^{\eta}(1+d(\text{x}_{\gamma},\text{x}_{\beta}))^{\eta}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle(\mkern-1.5mu2\mkern-1.5mu)}}{{\leq}}F_{\alpha,\beta}(\tau_{1}+\tau_{2})\sum_{\gamma}2^{\eta-1}\bigg[\frac{1}{(1+d(\text{x}_{\alpha},\text{x}_{\gamma}))^{\eta}}+\frac{1}{(1+d(\text{x}_{\gamma},\text{x}_{\beta}))^{\eta}}\bigg]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle(\mkern-1.5mu3\mkern-1.5mu)}}{{\leq}}B_{\eta}F_{\alpha,\beta}(\tau_{1}+\tau_{2}),

(115)

where in (1) we have used the triangle inequality (
d(\text{x}_{\alpha},\text{x}_{\beta})\leq d(\text{x}_{\alpha},\text{x}_{\gamma})+d(\text{x}_{\gamma},\text{x}_{\beta})
), in (2) we have used the inequality
(1+a+b)^{\eta}\leq((1+a)+(1+b))^{\eta}\leq 2^{\eta-1}((1+a)^{\eta}+(1+b)^{\eta})
for
a,b>0
and
\eta>1
, and in (3) we have used lemma
15
.
∎

Lemma 19

(Modified Lieb-Robinson recursion)
.

Suppose for
X\subseteq\Lambda
,
\nu_{X,\alpha}(t)\in[0,1]
satisfies the integral inequality

\displaystyle\nu_{X,\alpha}(\tau)\leq\nu_{X,\alpha}(0)+{\omega}_{0}\sum_{\beta}\int_{0}^{\tau}F_{\alpha,\beta}(\tau-\tau^{\prime})\nu_{X,\beta}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime},

(116)

where
\nu_{X,\alpha}(0)=0
if
X\cap\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}=\emptyset
,
\omega_{0}>0
and
F_{\alpha,\beta}(\tau)
is defined in Lemma
18
. Furthermore, suppose
\chi_{X,Y}(t)
satisfies the inequality

\displaystyle\chi_{X,Y}(\tau)\leq\omega_{1}\sum_{\alpha:\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}\cap Y\neq\emptyset}\int_{0}^{\tau}\nu_{X,\alpha}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}+\omega_{2}\sum_{\alpha^{\prime},\alpha:\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}\cap Y\neq\emptyset}\int_{0}^{\tau}F_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}(\tau-\tau^{\prime})\nu_{X,{\alpha^{\prime}}}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime},

(117)

with
\omega_{1},\omega_{2}\geq 0.

Then, assuming that
d(X,Y)>2a_{0}
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\chi_{X,Y}(\tau)\leq\chi_{0}\exp((\lambda_{E}+\omega_{0}B_{\eta})\tau-\kappa_{E}d(X,Y)/p_{+}),

(118)

where

\chi_{0}=\left|{X}\right|\left|{Y}\right|\mathcal{Z}^{2}\exp\bigg(\frac{\kappa_{E}(2a_{0}+q_{+})}{p_{+}}\bigg)\bigg[\frac{\omega_{1}\omega_{0}}{(\lambda_{E}+\omega_{0}B_{\eta})^{2}}+\frac{\omega_{2}}{\omega_{0}B_{\eta}+\lambda_{E}}\bigg].

Proof.

We can directly solve the recursion for
\nu_{X,\alpha}
: Recursing over Eq. (
116
), we obtain that

\nu_{X,{\alpha}}(\tau)\leq\nu_{X,{\alpha}}(0)+\sum_{\beta}\int_{0}^{\tau}G_{\alpha,\beta}(\tau-\tau^{\prime})\nu_{X,{\beta}}(0)d\tau^{\prime},

(119)

where

\displaystyle G_{\alpha,\beta}(\tau)

\displaystyle={\omega}_{0}F_{\alpha,\beta}(\tau)+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}{\omega_{0}}^{n+1}\sum_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}\cdots,\gamma_{n}}\int_{\tau\geq s_{1}\geq s_{2}\geq\cdots\geq s_{n}\geq 0}F_{\alpha,\gamma_{1}}(\tau-s_{1})F_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}}(s_{1}-s_{2})\cdots F_{\gamma_{n},\beta}(s_{n})ds_{1}ds_{2}\cdots ds_{n}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle(\mkern-1.5mu1\mkern-1.5mu)}}{{\leq}}{\omega}_{0}F_{\alpha,\beta}(\tau)+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}{\omega_{0}}^{n+1}B_{\eta}^{n}F_{\alpha,\beta}(\tau)\int_{\tau\geq s_{1}\geq s_{2}\geq\cdots\geq s_{n}\geq 0}ds_{1}ds_{2}\dots ds_{n}

\displaystyle\leq{\omega}_{0}F_{\alpha,\beta}(\tau)+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}{\omega}_{0}^{n+1}B_{\eta}^{n}F_{\alpha,\beta}(\tau)\frac{\tau^{n}}{n!}

\displaystyle={\omega}_{0}F_{\alpha,\beta}(\tau)\exp(\omega_{0}B_{\eta}\tau).

(120)

where in (1) we have used lemma
18
. Next, substituting Eq. (
119
) into the inequality for
\chi_{X,Y}(\tau)
and using the fact that
\nu_{X,\alpha}(0)=0
if
X\cap\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}=\emptyset
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\chi_{X,Y}(\tau)\leq\omega_{1}\bigg[\sum_{\alpha:\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}\cap Y\neq\emptyset}\nu_{X,\alpha}(0)\tau+\sum_{\alpha,\beta:\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}\cap Y\neq\emptyset}\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{0}^{\tau^{\prime}}G_{\alpha,\beta}(\tau^{\prime}-\tau^{\prime\prime})\nu_{X,\beta}(0)d\tau^{\prime\prime}d\tau^{\prime}\bigg]+

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\ \ \omega_{2}\bigg[\sum_{\alpha^{\prime},\alpha:\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}\cap Y\neq\emptyset}\int_{0}^{\tau}F_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}(\tau-\tau^{\prime})\nu_{X,\alpha^{\prime}}(0)d\tau^{\prime}\bigg.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\bigg.+\sum_{\alpha^{\prime},\alpha,\beta:\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}\cap Y\neq\emptyset}\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{0}^{\tau^{\prime}}F_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}(\tau-\tau^{\prime})G_{\alpha^{\prime},\beta}(\tau^{\prime}-\tau^{\prime\prime})\nu_{X,\beta}(0)d\tau^{\prime\prime}d\tau^{\prime}\bigg].

(121)

We now analyze the right hand side of this inequality term by term. Note that since
d(X,Y)>2a_{0}
, there is no
\alpha
such that
\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}\cap X\neq\emptyset
and
\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}\cap Y\neq\emptyset
. Thus,

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha:\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}\cap Y\neq\emptyset}\nu_{X,\alpha}(0)\tau\leq\sum_{\alpha:\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}\cap Y\neq\emptyset,\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}\cap X\neq\emptyset}\tau=0.

(122)

Next, note that if
\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}\cap X\neq\emptyset
and
\mathcal{S}_{\beta}\cap Y\neq\emptyset
, then
d(\mathcal{S}_{\alpha},\mathcal{S}_{\beta})\geq d(X,Y)-2a_{0}
and therefore, from Eq. (
100
),
d(\text{x}_{\alpha},\text{x}_{\beta})\geq(d(X,Y)-2a_{0}-q_{+})/p_{+}
. This implies

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha:\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}\cap Y\neq\emptyset}\ \sum_{\beta:\mathcal{S}_{\beta}\cap X\neq\emptyset}\frac{\exp(-\kappa_{E}d(\text{x}_{\alpha},\text{x}_{\beta}))}{(1+d(\text{x}_{\alpha},\text{x}_{\beta}))^{\eta}}\leq\left|{X}\right|\left|{Y}\right|\mathcal{Z}^{2}\exp(\kappa_{E}(2a_{0}+q_{+})/p_{+})\exp(-\kappa_{E}d(X,Y)/p_{+}).

(123)

It then follows that

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha,\beta:\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}\cap Y\neq\emptyset}\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{0}^{\tau^{\prime}}G_{\alpha,\beta}(\tau^{\prime}-\tau^{\prime\prime})\nu_{X,\beta}(0)d\tau^{\prime\prime}d\tau^{\prime}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\omega_{0}\sum_{\alpha:\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}\cap Y\neq\emptyset}\sum_{\beta:\mathcal{S}_{\beta}\cap X\neq\emptyset}\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{0}^{\tau^{\prime}}\frac{\exp((\lambda_{E}+\omega_{0}B_{\eta})(\tau^{\prime}-\tau^{\prime\prime})-\kappa_{E}d(\text{x}_{\alpha},\text{x}_{\beta}))}{(1+d(\text{x}_{\alpha},\text{x}_{\beta}))^{\eta}}d\tau^{\prime\prime}d\tau^{\prime}

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{\omega_{0}[\exp((\lambda_{E}+\omega_{0}B_{\eta})\tau)-1-(\lambda_{E}+\omega_{0}B_{\eta})\tau]}{(\lambda_{E}+\omega_{0}B_{\eta})^{2}}\sum_{\alpha:\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}\cap Y\neq\emptyset}\sum_{\beta:\mathcal{S}_{\beta}\cap X\neq\emptyset}\frac{\exp(-\kappa_{E}d(\text{x}_{\alpha},\text{x}_{\beta}))}{(1+d(\text{x}_{\alpha},\text{x}_{\beta}))^{\eta}}

\displaystyle\qquad\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle(\mkern-1.5mu1\mkern-1.5mu)}}{{\leq}}\left|{X}\right|\left|{Y}\right|\mathcal{Z}^{2}\frac{\omega_{0}\exp(\kappa_{E}(2a_{0}+q_{+})/p_{+})}{(\lambda_{E}+\omega_{0}B_{\eta})^{2}}\exp((\lambda_{E}+\omega_{0}B_{\eta})\tau)\exp(-\kappa_{E}d(X,Y)/p_{+}),

(124)

where in (1) we have used Eq. (
123
). A similar analysis would also yield that

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha^{\prime},\alpha:\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}\cap Y\neq\emptyset}\int_{0}^{\tau}F_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}(\tau-\tau^{\prime})\nu_{X,\alpha^{\prime}}(0)d\tau^{\prime}\leq\left|{X}\right|\left|{Y}\right|\mathcal{Z}^{2}\frac{\exp(\kappa_{E}(2a_{0}+q_{+})/p_{+})}{\lambda_{E}}(\exp(\lambda_{E}\tau)-1)\exp(-\kappa_{E}d(X,Y)/p_{+}).

(125)

Finally, consider

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha^{\prime},\alpha,\beta:\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}\cap Y\neq\emptyset}\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{0}^{\tau^{\prime}}F_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}(\tau-\tau^{\prime})G_{\alpha^{\prime},\beta}(\tau^{\prime}-\tau^{\prime\prime})\nu_{X,\beta}(0)d\tau^{\prime\prime}d\tau^{\prime}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\omega_{0}\sum_{\alpha:\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}\cap Y\neq\emptyset}\sum_{\beta:\mathcal{S}_{\beta}\cap X\neq\emptyset}\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}}\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{0}^{\tau^{\prime}}\exp(\omega_{0}B_{\eta}(\tau^{\prime}-\tau^{\prime\prime}))F_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}(\tau-\tau^{\prime})F_{\alpha^{\prime},\beta}(\tau^{\prime}-\tau^{\prime\prime})d\tau^{\prime\prime}d\tau^{\prime}

\displaystyle\qquad\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle(\mkern-1.5mu1\mkern-1.5mu)}}{{\leq}}\omega_{0}B_{\eta}\sum_{\alpha:\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}\cap Y\neq\emptyset}\sum_{\beta:\mathcal{S}_{\beta}\cap X\neq\emptyset}\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{0}^{\tau^{\prime}}\exp(\omega_{0}B_{\eta}(\tau^{\prime}-\tau^{\prime\prime}))F_{\alpha,\beta}(\tau-\tau^{\prime\prime})d\tau^{\prime\prime}d\tau^{\prime}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\sum_{\alpha:\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}\cap Y\neq\emptyset}\sum_{\beta:\mathcal{S}_{\beta}\cap X\neq\emptyset}\int_{0}^{\tau}[\exp(\omega_{0}B_{\eta}(\tau-\tau^{\prime}))-1]F_{\alpha,\beta}(\tau-\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\left[\frac{\exp((\lambda_{E}+\omega_{0}B_{\eta})\tau)-1}{\lambda_{E}+\omega_{0}B_{\eta}}-\frac{\exp(\lambda_{E}\tau)-1}{\lambda_{E}}\right]\sum_{\alpha:\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}\cap Y\neq\emptyset}\sum_{\beta:\mathcal{S}_{\beta}\cap X\neq\emptyset}\frac{\exp(-\kappa_{E}d(\textnormal{x}_{\alpha},\textnormal{x}_{\beta}))}{(1+d(\textnormal{x}_{\alpha},\textnormal{x}_{\beta}))^{\eta}}

\displaystyle\qquad\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle(\mkern-1.5mu2\mkern-1.5mu)}}{{\leq}}\left|{X}\right|\left|{Y}\right|\mathcal{Z}^{2}\exp(\kappa_{E}(2a_{0}+q_{+})/p_{+})\left[\frac{\exp((\lambda_{E}+\omega_{0}B_{\eta})\tau)-1}{\lambda_{E}+\omega_{0}B_{\eta}}-\frac{\exp(\lambda_{E}\tau)-1}{\lambda_{E}}\right]\exp(-\kappa_{E}d(X,Y)/p_{+}),

(126)

where in (1) we have used lemma
18
and in (2) we have used Eq. (
123
).

Combining Eqs. (
121
), (
122
), (
124
), (
125
) and (
126
) and combining the two terms proportional to
\omega_{2}
, we obtain the lemma statement.
∎

Lemma 20
.

For the coupled bath model, it follows that

\gamma_{\bm{\zeta},\mathcal{B}}^{X,Y}(t,t^{\prime})\leq\left|{X}\right|\left|{Y}\right|\Gamma_{0}(t)\exp\left(\frac{\kappa_{E}}{p_{+}}\bigg({v_{\textnormal{LR}}(\bm{\zeta},t)(t-t^{\prime})-d(X,Y)}\bigg)\right),

where
v_{\textnormal{LR}}(\bm{\zeta},t)
and
\Gamma_{0}(t)
are given by

\displaystyle v_{\textnormal{LR}}(\bm{\zeta},t)=v_{0}+V_{0}(\|{\bm{\zeta}_{[0,t]}}\|_{\infty}+2\|{\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{R}}(-t,t)}\|_{\infty})

with

\displaystyle v_{0}=\frac{p_{+}\lambda_{E}}{\kappa_{E}}+\frac{2p_{+}B_{\eta}\exp(\kappa_{E}q_{-}+2\lambda_{E}\delta)}{\kappa_{E}\min(\lambda_{0},1-\lambda_{0}q_{-})^{\eta}}(1+2K_{0}\mathcal{Z})\quad\text{and}\quad V_{0}=\frac{4p_{+}B_{\eta}\exp(\kappa_{E}q_{-}+2\lambda_{E}\delta)}{\kappa_{E}\min(\lambda_{0},1-\lambda_{0}q_{-})^{\eta}},

and

\displaystyle\Gamma_{0}(t)=\exp\bigg(\frac{2\kappa_{E}a_{0}}{p_{+}}\bigg)+\mathcal{Z}^{2}\exp\bigg(\frac{\kappa_{E}(2a_{0}+q_{+})}{p_{+}}\bigg)\bigg[\frac{\omega(t)\tilde{\omega}(t)}{\lambda_{E}^{2}}+\frac{\tilde{\omega}_{1}(t)}{\lambda_{E}}\bigg],

with

\displaystyle\omega(t)=2(1+2\|{\bm{\zeta}_{[0,t]}}\|_{\infty}+4\|{\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{R}}(-t,t)}\|_{\infty}),

and

\displaystyle\tilde{\omega}(t)=\frac{\exp(\kappa_{E}q_{-}+2\lambda_{E}\delta)}{\min(\lambda_{0},1-\lambda_{0}q_{-})^{\eta}}\left(\omega(t)+4\mathcal{Z}K_{0}\right)\quad\text{and}\quad\tilde{\omega}_{1}(t)=4K_{0}\frac{\exp(2\lambda_{E}\delta)}{(\min(1,\lambda_{0}))^{\eta}}

Proof.

Setting
Y=S_{\alpha}
in Lemma
17
yields

\Gamma_{X,S_{\alpha}}(\tau)\leq\Gamma_{X,S_{\alpha}}(0)+\omega(t)\sum_{\beta:S_{\beta}\cap S_{\alpha}\neq\emptyset}\int_{0}^{\tau}\Gamma_{X,S_{\beta}}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}+2\sum_{\alpha^{\prime},\beta,\sigma:S_{\beta}\cap\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}\neq\emptyset}\int_{0}^{\tau}\Gamma_{X,S_{\alpha^{\prime}}}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}\int_{0}^{\tau-\tau^{\prime}}\left|{K^{\delta}_{\alpha^{\prime},\beta,\sigma}(\tau^{\prime\prime})}\right|d\tau^{\prime\prime},

(127)

where

\omega(t)=2(1+2\|{\bm{\zeta}_{[0,t]}}\|_{\infty}+4\|{\bm{\mu}^{\textnormal{R}}(-t,t)}\|_{\infty}).

(128)

We can write the above expression in a more compact form as

\Gamma_{X,S_{\alpha}}(\tau)\leq\Gamma_{X,S_{\alpha}}(0)+\sum_{\beta}\int_{0}^{\tau}f_{\alpha,\beta}(\tau-\tau^{\prime})\Gamma_{X,S_{\beta}}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime},

(129)

where

f_{\alpha,\beta}(\tau)=\omega(t)\Theta(S_{\beta}\cap S_{\alpha}\neq\emptyset)+2\sum_{\beta^{\prime},\sigma:S_{\beta^{\prime}}\cap S_{\alpha}\neq\emptyset}\int_{0}^{\tau}\left|{K^{\delta}_{\beta,\beta^{\prime},\sigma}(\tau^{\prime})}\right|d\tau^{\prime}.

(130)

Next, we derive a simpler upper bound on
f_{\alpha,\beta}(\tau)
which allows us to use lemma
19
. Note that if
S_{\beta^{\prime}}\cap S_{\alpha}\neq\emptyset
, the
d(S_{\alpha},S_{\beta^{\prime}})=0
: Therefore, from Eq. (
100
), we obtain
d(\text{x}_{\alpha},\text{x}_{\beta^{\prime}})\leq q_{-}
.
Consequently, for any
\beta^{\prime}
,

d(\text{x}_{\beta},\text{x}_{\beta^{\prime}})\geq d(\text{x}_{\beta},\text{x}_{\alpha})-d(\text{x}_{\beta^{\prime}},\text{x}_{\alpha})\geq d(\text{x}_{\beta},\text{x}_{\alpha})-q_{-},

(131)

and hence

\displaystyle\sum_{\beta^{\prime},\sigma:S_{\beta^{\prime}}\cap S_{\alpha}\neq\emptyset}\int_{0}^{\tau}\left|{K^{\delta}_{\beta,\beta^{\prime},\sigma}(\tau^{\prime})}\right|d\tau^{\prime}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle(\mkern-1.5mu1\mkern-1.5mu)}}{{\leq}}\sum_{\beta^{\prime},\sigma:S_{\beta^{\prime}}\cap S_{\alpha}\neq\emptyset}\text{TV}(K_{\beta,\beta^{\prime},\sigma};[-(\tau+2\delta),(\tau+2\delta)])

(132)

\displaystyle\leq K_{0}\frac{\exp\left(\lambda_{E}(\tau+2\delta)-\kappa_{E}(d(\text{x}_{\beta},\text{x}_{\alpha})-q_{-})\right)}{\left(1+\lambda_{0}(d(\text{x}_{\beta},\text{x}_{\alpha})-q_{-})\right)^{\eta}}\sum_{\beta^{\prime},\sigma:S_{\beta^{\prime}}\cap S_{\alpha}\neq\emptyset}1

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle(\mkern-1.5mu2\mkern-1.5mu)}}{{\leq}}2\mathcal{Z}K_{0}\frac{\exp(\kappa_{E}q_{-}+2\lambda_{E}\delta)}{\min(\lambda_{0},1-\lambda_{0}q_{-})^{\eta}}F_{\alpha,\beta}(\tau),

where
F_{\alpha,\beta}(\tau)
is defined in lemma
18
. In (1), we have used lemma
2
and in (2), we have used that
\lambda_{0}q_{-}<1
which can be satisfied as per lemma
14
. Furthermore, note that

\displaystyle\Theta(S_{\alpha}\cap S_{\beta}\neq\emptyset)\leq\frac{\exp(\lambda_{E}(\tau+2\delta)-\kappa_{E}p_{-}d(S_{\alpha},S_{\beta}))}{(1+\lambda_{0}p_{-}d(S_{\alpha},S_{\beta}))^{\eta}},

(133)

since if
\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}\cap\mathcal{S}_{\beta}\neq\emptyset
or, equivalently,
d(S_{\alpha},S_{\beta})=0
then the left-hand side is 1 while the right-hand side is
\exp(\lambda_{E}(\tau+2\delta))>1
while if
\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}\cap\mathcal{S}_{\beta}=\emptyset
then the left-hand side is 0 while the right-hand side is non-negative. Using Eq. (
100
), we obtain that
p_{-}d(S_{\alpha},S_{\beta})\geq d(\text{x}_{\alpha},\text{x}_{\beta})-q_{-}
and thus

\displaystyle\Theta(S_{\alpha}\cap S_{\beta}\neq\emptyset)\leq\frac{\exp(\kappa_{E}q_{-}+2\lambda_{E}\delta)}{\min(\lambda_{0},1-\lambda_{0}q_{-})^{\eta}}F_{\alpha,\beta}(\tau).

(134)

Combining the upper bounds derived above, we then obtain

f_{\alpha,\beta}(\tau)\leq\tilde{\omega}(t)F_{\alpha,\beta}(\tau)\quad\text{where}\quad\tilde{\omega}(t)=\frac{\exp(\kappa_{E}q_{-}+2\lambda_{E}\delta)}{\min(\lambda_{0},1-\lambda_{0}q_{-})^{\eta}}\left(\omega(t)+4\mathcal{Z}K_{0}\right).

(135)

Thus, Eq. (
129
) can be rewritten as

\displaystyle\Gamma_{X,S_{\alpha}}(\tau)\leq\Gamma_{X,S_{\alpha}}(0)+\tilde{\omega}(t)\sum_{\beta}\int_{0}^{\tau}F_{\alpha,\beta}(\tau-\tau^{\prime})\Gamma_{X,S_{\beta}}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}.

(136)

Next, we note that if
\tau>0
, then

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\tau}|{K_{\alpha^{\prime},\alpha,\sigma}^{\delta}(s)}|ds\leq\text{TV}(K_{\alpha^{\prime},\alpha,\sigma};[-(\tau+2\delta),(\tau+2\delta)])\leq K_{0}\frac{\exp(\lambda_{E}(\tau+2\delta)-\kappa_{E}d(\text{x}_{\alpha},\text{x}_{\alpha^{\prime}}))}{(1+\lambda_{0}d(\text{x}_{\alpha},\text{x}_{\alpha^{\prime}}))^{\eta}}\leq\frac{\tilde{\omega}_{1}(t)}{4}F_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}(\tau),

(137)

where
\tilde{\omega}_{1}(t)=4K_{0}\exp(2\lambda_{E}\delta)/(\min(1,\lambda_{0}))^{\eta}
. Thus from lemma
17
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\Gamma_{X,Y}(\tau)\leq\Gamma_{X,Y}(0)+\omega(t)\sum_{\alpha:S_{\alpha}\cap Y\neq\emptyset}\int_{0}^{\tau}\Gamma_{X,S_{\alpha}}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}+\tilde{\omega}_{1}(t)\sum_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}:S_{\alpha}\cap Y\neq\emptyset}\int_{0}^{\tau}F_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}(\tau-\tau^{\prime})\Gamma_{X,S_{\alpha^{\prime}}}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}.

(138)

For
d(X,Y)>2a_{0}
, using lemma
19
with Eqs. (
136
) and (
138
), we obtain that

\displaystyle\Gamma_{X,Y}(\tau)\leq\left|{X}\right|\left|{Y}\right|\Gamma(t)\exp\bigg(\frac{\kappa_{E}}{p_{+}}(v_{\text{LR}}(\bm{\zeta},t)\tau-d(X,Y))\bigg),

(139a)

where

\displaystyle v_{\text{LR}}(\bm{\zeta},t)=v_{0}+V_{0}\big(\|{\bm{\zeta}_{[0,t]}}\|_{\infty}+2\|{\bm{\mu}^{\text{R}}(-t,t)}\|_{\infty}\big)

(139b)

with

\displaystyle v_{0}=\frac{p_{+}\lambda_{E}}{\kappa_{E}}+\frac{2p_{+}B_{\eta}\exp(\kappa_{E}q_{-}+2\lambda_{E}\delta)}{\kappa_{E}\min(\lambda_{0},1-\lambda_{0}q_{-})^{\eta}}(1+2K_{0}\mathcal{Z})\quad\text{and}\quad V_{0}=\frac{4p_{+}B_{\eta}\exp(\kappa_{E}q_{-}+2\lambda_{E}\delta)}{\kappa_{E}\min(\lambda_{0},1-\lambda_{0}q_{-})^{\eta}},

and

\displaystyle\Gamma(t)=\mathcal{Z}^{2}\exp\bigg(\frac{\kappa_{E}(2a_{0}+q_{+})}{p_{+}}\bigg)\bigg[\frac{\omega(t)\tilde{\omega}(t)}{(\lambda_{E}+\tilde{\omega}(t)B_{\eta})^{2}}+\frac{\tilde{\omega}_{1}(t)}{\tilde{\omega}(t)B_{\eta}+\lambda_{E}}\bigg].

(139c)

To obtain the precise bound in the lemma statement, we note that
\gamma^{X,Y}_{\bm{\zeta}}(t,t^{\prime})=\Gamma_{X,Y}(t-t^{\prime})
. Furthermore,
\tilde{\omega}(t)\geq 0
implies
\Gamma(t)\leq\Gamma_{0}(t)
, where
\Gamma_{0}(t)
is defined in the lemma statement. For
d(X,Y)\leq 2a_{0}
, the claimed bound follows from
\gamma^{X,Y}_{\bm{\zeta},\mathcal{B}}(t,t^{\prime})\leq 1
and
\Gamma_{0}(t)\geq\exp(2\kappa_{E}a_{0}/p_{+})
. Thus the estimate holds for all
X
and
Y
.

∎

Proof of proposition 2.

We will again analyze the observable error
\Delta_{O_{X}}(t,t^{\prime};l)
for the
\delta-
mollified model, and take the limit of
\delta\to 0
at the end. By Lemma
5
, for
t>t^{\prime}
the truncation error decomposes as

\Delta_{O_{X}}(t,t^{\prime};l)\leq\Delta_{O_{X}}^{\text{R}}(t,t^{\prime};l)+\Delta^{\text{L}}_{O_{X}}(t,t^{\prime};l).

We will separately upper-bound the both the terms on the right-hand side of this inequality. It will be convenient to define
v_{\text{LR},c}(t)
via

v_{\text{LR},c}(t)=v_{0}+6V_{0}C_{E}(t+2\delta)^{d}+6V_{0}B_{E},

with
v_{0}
and
V_{0}
being as defined in lemma
20
and
B_{E},C_{E}
are defined in lemma
16
. We remark that
v_{\text{LR},c}(t)=\Theta(t^{d})
as
t\to\infty
. Furthermore, since from lemma
16
,
\|{\bm{\mu}^{\text{R}}_{[0,t]}}\|_{\infty}\leq 2(C_{E}(t+2\delta)^{d}+B_{E})
, it follows that the Lieb-Robinson velocity defined in lemma
20
satisfies

v_{\text{LR}}(\bm{\zeta},t)\leq v_{\text{LR},c}(t)\quad\text{for}\quad\bm{\zeta}=\bm{\mu}_{[0,s]}^{\text{R}}\quad\text{with}\quad 0\leq s\leq t.

Similarly, we define

\displaystyle\Gamma_{0,c}(t)={}

\displaystyle\exp\bigg(\frac{2\kappa_{E}a_{0}}{p_{+}}\bigg)

\displaystyle+\frac{\mathcal{Z}^{2}\exp(\kappa_{E}(2a_{0}+q_{+})/p_{+}+\kappa_{E}q_{-}+2\lambda_{E}\delta)}{(\min(1,\lambda_{0},1-\lambda_{0}q_{-}))^{\eta}}\bigg[\frac{\omega_{c}^{2}(t)+4K_{0}\mathcal{Z}\omega_{c}(t)}{\lambda_{E}^{2}}+\frac{4K_{0}}{\lambda_{E}}\bigg]

\displaystyle\text{where}

\displaystyle\omega_{c}(t)=2+24(C_{E}(t+2\delta)^{d}+B_{E}),

and remark that, also as a consequence of lemma
16
,
\Gamma_{0}(t)\leq\Gamma_{0,c}(t)
. Furthermore, it can be noted that
\Gamma_{0,c}(t)=\Theta(t^{2d})
as
t\to\infty
.

In terms of
v_{\text{LR},c}(t)
and
\Gamma_{0,c}(t)
, we can then easily see that lemma
20
implies that for all
s\in[0,t]
and
t>t^{\prime}
,

\displaystyle\gamma^{X,Y}_{\bm{\mu}^{\text{R}}_{[0,s]},\mathcal{A}_{X[l]}}(t,t^{\prime})\leq\left|{X}\right|\left|{Y}\right|\Gamma_{0,c}(t)\exp\bigg(\frac{\kappa_{E}}{p_{+}}(v_{\text{LR},c}(t)(t-t^{\prime})-d(X,Y))\bigg).

(140)

Upper bounding
\Delta_{O_{X}}^{\text{R}}(t,t^{\prime};l)
. From lemma
8
,

\Delta_{O_{X}}^{\text{R}}(t,t^{\prime},l)\leq 2\left\|{O_{X}}\right\|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\notin\mathcal{A}_{X[l]}\\
S_{\alpha}\cap X_{c}[l]{\neq\emptyset}\end{subarray}}(1+\mu^{\textnormal{R}}_{\alpha}(0,t))\int_{s=t^{\prime}}^{t}\gamma_{\bm{\mu}_{[0,s]}^{R},\mathcal{A}_{X[l]}}^{X,S_{\alpha}}(t,s)ds.

(141)

From lemma
16
, it follows that
